For each integer \(n\ge2\), let \(F_n\) be the free group on \(n\) generators. For any group \(G\), define its lower central series by \(\gamma_1(G)=G\) and \(\gamma_{r+1}(G)=[\gamma_r(G),G]\), where \([H,K]\) is the subgroup generated by the commutators \([h,k]=hkh^{-1}k^{-1}\) with \(h\in H\) and \(k\in K\). For every integer \(r\ge1\), define
\[
\mathcal A_r(n)=\ker\!\left(\operatorname{Aut}(F_n)\longrightarrow\operatorname{Aut}\bigl(F_n/\gamma_{r+1}(F_n)\bigr)\right),
\qquad \operatorname{IA}_n=\mathcal A_1(n).
\]
Determine, by explicit conditions on \(n\) and \(c\), the complete set
\[
\mathcal D=\left\{(n,c)\in\mathbb Z_{\ge2}\times\mathbb Z_{\ge1}:\ 
\gamma_{c+1}(\operatorname{IA}_n)\subsetneq
\gamma_c(\operatorname{IA}_n)\cap\mathcal A_{c+1}(n)
\right\}.
\]

```
[exit code 0, 0.1s]
(ran it after writing 3 lines)
--- stdout ---
VERIFIED EXCLUSIONS
(1) For every n>=2, (n,1) is not in D.
Magnus generators for IA_n are K_ij (xi -> xj xi xj^-1) and K_i;jk (xi -> xi[xj,xk], j<k distinct from i), fixing the other basis elements. Their number is N=n^2(n-1)/2. The degree-one Johnson map tau: IA_n -> Hom(H,L_2(H)) sends these generators to a signed Z-basis. Consequently the surjection Z^N -> IA_n^ab induced by this generating set is an isomorphism: its composite with tau_ab is a signed permutation isomorphism. Thus ker(tau)=gamma_2(IA_n). By the definition of tau its kernel is A_2(n), proving the assertion.

(2) For every c>=1, (2,c) is not in D.
Nielsen's theorem gives IA_2=Inn(F_2), and F_2 has trivial center. Thus gamma_r(IA_2)=Inn(gamma_r(F_2)). The center of F_2/gamma_{r+1}(F_2) is gamma_r/gamma_{r+1}. To see this, if a central class has a representative with first nonzero lower-central homogeneous term P of degree d<r, the Magnus-Witt embedding of the free Lie algebra into the tensor algebra implies [P,X_i]=0 for every basis letter X_i. The centralizer of X_i in the free associative algebra is Z[X_i], and the intersections for two distinct letters consist of scalars. Thus P=0, contradiction. (The centralizer assertion follows by comparing coefficients in PX_i=X_iP: any monomial not a power of X_i would generate infinitely many distinct monomials with the same nonzero coefficient, impossible for a polynomial.) Hence an inner automorphism by w belongs to A_r iff w is in gamma_r(F_2), proving A_r(2)=gamma_r(IA_2). Consequently the intersection defining D equals gamma_{c+1} for rank2.

COMPUTATIONAL CHECKS
The companion file agent2_low_degree_checks.py uses actual degree-two Magnus expansions to check the signed-basis assertion for 2<=n<=7. It checks the zero-center free-Lie assertion by exact rational ranks for n=2,1<=degree<=7 and n=3,1<=degree<=4. Output signed-basis dimensions 2,9,24,50,90,147; Lie dimensions rank2 2,1,2,3,6,9,18 and rank3 3,3,8,18.

"""Independent finite checks for low-degree facts used in the Andreadakis problem.
Run with Python. Uses exact integer/rational arithmetic only.
These tests corroborate, and do not replace, the general mathematical arguments.
"""
from itertools import product
from math import prod
import sympy as S

def add(a,b,scale=1):
    c=dict(a)
    for w,v in b.items():
        c[w]=c.get(w,0)+scale*v
        if not c[w]: del c[w]
    return c

def mul(a,b,N):
    c={}
    for u,x in a.items():
        for v,y in b.items():
            if len(u)+len(v)<=N:
                w=u+v;c[w]=c.get(w,0)+x*y
    return {w:v for w,v in c.items() if v}

def bracket(a,b):
    N=max(map(len,a),default=0)+max(map(len,b),default=0)
    return add(mul(a,b,N),mul(b,a,N),-1)

def magnus(word,N):
    a={():1}
    for k in word:
        i=abs(k)-1
        b={():1,(i,):1} if k>0 else {tuple([i]*d):(-1)**d for d in range(N+1)}
        a=mul(a,b,N)
    return a

def inverse(word):return tuple(-x for x in word[::-1])
def comm(u,v):return tuple(u)+tuple(v)+inverse(u)+inverse(v)

def johnson1(image,i):
    a=magnus(tuple(image)+(-i,),2)
    return {w:v for w,v in a.items() if len(w)==2}

def check_magnus_generators(n):
    # Basis coordinate is (input, ordered pair a<b).
    labels=[(i,a,b) for i in range(1,n+1) for a in range(1,n+1) for b in range(a+1,n+1)]
    columns=[]
    for i in range(1,n+1):
        for j in range(1,n+1):
            if j==i:continue
            im=(j,i,-j)
            d=johnson1(im,i)
            columns.append([d.get((a-1,b-1),0) if k==i else 0 for k,a,b in labels])
        for j in range(1,n+1):
            for k in range(j+1,n+1):
                if i in (j,k):continue
                im=(i,)+comm((j,),(k,))
                d=johnson1(im,i)
                columns.append([d.get((a-1,b-1),0) if q==i else 0 for q,a,b in labels])
    M=S.Matrix.hstack(*(S.Matrix(c) for c in columns))
    assert M.rows==M.cols==n*n*(n-1)//2
    # In fact a signed permutation matrix, so no expensive determinant needed.
    assert all(sum(abs(M[i,j]) for i in range(M.rows))==1 for j in range(M.cols))
    assert all(sum(abs(M[i,j]) for j in range(M.cols))==1 for i in range(M.rows))
    return M.rows

def lyndon(w):return bool(w) and all(w<w[i:] for i in range(1,len(w)))

def lie_word(w):
    assert lyndon(w)
    if len(w)==1:return {w:1}
    for i in range(1,len(w)):
        if lyndon(w[i:]):
            return bracket(lie_word(w[:i]),lie_word(w[i:]))
    raise AssertionError

def check_center(n,d):
    W=[w for w in product(range(n),repeat=d) if lyndon(w)]
    bases=[lie_word(w) for w in W]
    witt=sum(int(S.mobius(k))*n**(d//k) for k in S.divisors(d))//d
    assert len(W)==witt
    rows=[(i,w) for i in range(n) for w in product(range(n),repeat=d+1)]
    cols=[]
    for b in bases:
        images=[bracket(b,{(i,):1}) for i in range(n)]
        cols.append([images[i].get(w,0) for i,w in rows])
    A=S.Matrix.hstack(*(S.Matrix(v) for v in cols))
    assert A.rank()==len(W)
    return len(W)

if __name__=='__main__':
    for n in range(2,8):
        print('Johnson1 signed basis',n,check_magnus_generators(n))
    for n,maxdegree in [(2,7),(3,4)]:
        for d in range(1,maxdegree+1):
            print('zero free-Lie center',n,d,check_center(n,d))


--- stderr ---
(empty)
```

The following exclusions are verified:
\[
\mathcal D\cap\bigl(\{2\}\times\mathbb Z_{\ge1}\bigr)=\varnothing,
\qquad
\mathcal D\cap\bigl(\mathbb Z_{\ge2}\times\{1\}\bigr)=\varnothing.
\]

Indeed:

* **Rank two.** Nielsen’s theorem gives \(\operatorname{IA}_2=\operatorname{Inn}(F_2)\). Since
  \[
  Z(F_2/\gamma_{r+1}(F_2))=\gamma_r(F_2)/\gamma_{r+1}(F_2),
  \]
  one obtains
  \[
  \mathcal A_r(2)=\operatorname{Inn}(\gamma_r(F_2))
  =\gamma_r(\operatorname{IA}_2).
  \]
  Consequently none of the required inclusions is strict.

* **Degree one.** The first Johnson homomorphism induces the Magnus abelianization isomorphism
  \[
  \operatorname{IA}_n^{\mathrm{ab}}
  \cong H^*\otimes\Lambda^2H,\qquad H=F_n^{\mathrm{ab}}.
  \]
  Its kernel is \(\mathcal A_2(n)\), so
  \(\mathcal A_2(n)=\gamma_2(\operatorname{IA}_n)\).

More generally, the problem is precisely to determine when
\[
\ker\!\left(
\frac{\gamma_c(\operatorname{IA}_n)}
     {\gamma_{c+1}(\operatorname{IA}_n)}
\longrightarrow
\operatorname{Hom}\!\left(H,\gamma_{c+1}(F_n)/\gamma_{c+2}(F_n)\right)
\right)
\ne0.
\]
Computing the image of this map—even by exact arithmetic—does **not** determine its kernel without information about the source. Our computations did not bridge that gap.

A possible source of confusion is the recent work on the **McCool groups** of basis-conjugating automorphisms. Those groups are not \(\operatorname{IA}_n\), and their graded-kernel results cannot simply be transferred to the group in this question.

I cannot substantiate the claimed recent complete theorem for \(\operatorname{IA}_n\). A citation to that theorem would be needed for me to continue responsibly rather than invent the missing classification or proof.

```
import pickle,collections
x=pickle.load(open('workspace/agent4_candidate_tau8.pkl','rb'))
def necklace(w):return min(w[k:]+w[:k] for k in range(len(w)))
for side in ['left','right','all']:
 tr=collections.Counter()
 for (i,w),c in x.items():
  if side=='left' and w[0]==i:tr[necklace(w[1:])]+=c
  if side=='right' and w[-1]==i:tr[necklace(w[:-1])]+=c
  if side=='all':
   for k,j in enumerate(w):
    if i==j:tr[necklace(w[k+1:]+w[:k])]+=c
 tr={w:c for w,c in tr.items() if c}
 print(side,len(tr),'mod3',sum(c%3!=0 for c in tr.values()),list(tr.items())[:5])
```

```
from pathlib import Path
code=r'''"""Exact finite checks for support reduction of the degree-two Johnson kernel.
The universal proof is in agent1_support_lemma.txt. No modular rank inference.
"""
from itertools import product,combinations
from collections import defaultdict

def add(a,b,scale=1):
 c=dict(a)
 for w,v in b.items():
  c[w]=c.get(w,0)+scale*v
  if c[w]==0:del c[w]
 return c

def bracket(a,b):
 c=defaultdict(int)
 for u,x in a.items():
  for v,y in b.items():c[u+v]+=x*y;c[v+u]-=x*y
 return {w:x for w,x in c.items() if x}

def lyndon(w):return all(w<w[i:] for i in range(1,len(w)))
def lie(w):
 if len(w)==1:return {w:1}
 k=next(i for i in range(1,len(w)) if lyndon(w[i:]))
 return bracket(lie(w[:k]),lie(w[k:]))

def trace(i,p):
 c=defaultdict(int)
 for w,x in p.items():
  if w[0]==i:c[tuple(sorted(w[1:]))]+=x
 return {w:x for w,x in c.items() if x}

def deriv_apply(D,p):
 i,q=D;c=defaultdict(int)
 for w,x in p.items():
  for k,j in enumerate(w):
   if j==i:
    for v,y in q.items():c[w[:k]+v+w[k+1:]]+=x*y
 return {w:x for w,x in c.items() if x}

def deriv_bracket(D,E):
 i,p=D;j,q=E;c={}
 for k,v in [(j,deriv_apply(D,q)),(i,{w:-x for w,x in deriv_apply(E,p).items()})]:
  for w,x in v.items():
   key=(k,w);c[key]=c.get(key,0)+x
 return {w:x for w,x in c.items() if x}

def check(n):
 X=[{(i,):1} for i in range(n)]
 L3=[lie(w) for w in product(range(n),repeat=3) if lyndon(w)]
 traces=[]
 for i in range(n):
  for p in L3:
   t=trace(i,p)
   assert len(t)<=1 and all(abs(x)==1 for x in t.values())
   traces.append(t)
 for a in range(n):
  for b in range(a,n):
   if a!=b:r=a;p=bracket(bracket(X[a],X[b]),X[a])
   else:r=0 if a else 1;p=bracket(bracket(X[r],X[a]),X[a])
   t=trace(r,p)
   assert len(t)==1 and abs(t.get((a,b),0))==1
   assert p in L3 or {w:-x for w,x in p.items()} in L3
 V=[(i,bracket(X[a],X[b])) for i in range(n) for a,b in combinations(range(n),2)]
 for D,E in combinations(V,2):
  b=deriv_bracket(D,E)
  support={D[0],E[0]}|set().union(*(set(w) for w in D[1]))|set().union(*(set(w) for w in E[1]))
  if b:assert len(support)<=5
  t=defaultdict(int)
  for (i,w),x in b.items():
   if w[0]==i:t[tuple(sorted(w[1:]))]+=x
  assert all(x==0 for x in t.values())
 return len(L3),len(V),len(traces)
if __name__=='__main__':
 for n in range(2,8):print(n,check(n))
'''
Path('workspace/agent1_support_checks.py').write_text(code)
exec(compile(code,'workspace/agent1_support_checks.py','exec'))
```

```
from pathlib import Path
proof=r'''BOUNDED-SUPPORT LEMMA FOR THE SECOND JOHNSON BRACKET
Let H_n=Z^n, V_n=H_n^* tensor Lambda^2 H_n, W_n=H_n^* tensor L_3(H_n), and B_n:Lambda^2 V_n -> W_n the derivation bracket. Let T_n:W_n -> Sym^2 H_n be left tensor contraction followed by commutativization. Then:
(A) T_n B_n=0.
(B) If im B_m=ker T_m for 2<=m<=4, then im B_n=ker T_n for all n.
(C) Under the same assumption, ker B_n is generated, under coordinate inclusions, by the kernels ker B_m for 2<=m<=6.

PROOF. Use the ordinary Lyndon basis of L_3 and coordinate tensor bases everywhere. Each basis column of T_n is zero or plus/minus a single commutative quadratic monomial. This follows directly by expanding [[a,b],c] or [a,[b,c]]: after left contraction with i and symmetrization, the two contributions from i=c (respectively i=a) cancel, while either remaining term is a single monomial. Coefficients have magnitude1.

For each monomial ab with a<b select a distinguished coordinate column d_ab with trace ab: take output a, Lie word [[a,b],a], changing its sign if necessary. The Lie word is plus/minus a Lyndon basis element. It has support {a,b}. For square aa select output r and word [[r,a],a], where r=1 unless a=1, in which case r=2. This also is plus/minus a Lyndon word with trace aa.

An integral basis E of ker T_n consists of all zero-trace coordinate columns and, for every non-distinguished nonzero-trace coordinate column w with trace epsilon*ab, the difference w-epsilon*d_ab. Every member e of E is supported on at most4 labels: a coordinate W column uses at most4 labels, and if its trace is nonzero it uses at most3 labels (output must occur among the three inputs); subtracting d_ab adds at most1 label.

By the rank<=4 hypothesis, each e has an integral B-preimage supported on its own labels, padding to2 labels if needed. (There are no nonzero W columns on1 label.) Choose such a preimage and thereby define an integral section s:ker T_n -> Lambda^2 V_n. This proves (B). Assertion (A) is the direct derivation-bracket calculation, also independently finite-checked in agent1_support_checks.py.

For any coordinate basis column u=D wedge E of Lambda^2 V_n, its support S uses at most6 labels. If B_n(u) is nonzero, S uses at most5 labels, since nonzero substitution requires the output index of one derivation among the two inputs of the other. In the canonical ker T basis expansion of B_n(u), all non-distinguished coordinate columns use S. Distinguished off-diagonal trace preimages add no labels. Distinguished square preimages collectively add at most one new label: if 1 is not in S, they can add only1; if 1 is in S, they can add only2. Thus sB_n(u), and hence k_u=u-sB_n(u), use at most6 labels. If B_n(u)=0, then k_u=u already has support<=6. Each k_u lies in ker B_n.

Finally if z=sum a_u*u belongs to ker B_n, then z=sum a_u*(u-sB_n(u)), because sB_n(z)=0. Thus all ker B_n are generated by the k_u on at most6 labels, proving (C).

APPLICATION TO IA. Let R_n be the kernel of Lambda^2(IA_n^ab) -> gamma_2 IA_n/gamma_3 IA_n, using the first Johnson identification IA_n^ab=V_n. Then R_n is contained in ker B_n. If exact source certificates show R_m=ker B_m for m<=6, coordinate inclusions imply ker B_n subset R_n for all n, by (C). Hence the degree2 Johnson map is injective for all n, and (n,2) is excluded from D for all n. Agent2 has source certificates in ranks3..6; rank2 is classical IA_2=Inn(F_2). Exact im B_m=ker T_m for m<=4 is separately required and follows from the bracket ranks + surjectivity of T and primitivity (can verify unit-pivot image matrices).

The companion script does exact tensor arithmetic for n=2..7, verifying T-column form, distinguished preimages, T*B=0, and the nonzero bracket support bound. It is corroboration of the universal combinatorial proof, not a substitute for source relation certificates.
'''
Path('workspace/agent1_support_lemma.txt').write_text(proof)
print(proof)

```

```
--- stdout ---
# Integral injectivity in degree 2, all ranks

Let H=Z^n, V=H^* tensor Lambda^2 H, Q=Lambda^2 V, T=H^* tensor L_3(H), and B:Q->T be derivation bracket. Magnus generation and the first Johnson map identify IA_n^ab=V. Consequently the true relation lattice R_n=ker(Q->gr^gamma_2 IA_n) lies in ker B. Any actual group relation with zero exponent sums yields an element of R_n by taking its class-two free-group signature, and R_n is stable under GL_n(Z).

## Exact finite certificates
`agent2_source_relations.py` enumerates reduced words in the standard Magnus automorphisms. It represents their action by exact reduced free words, identifies equal automorphisms, and records differences of class-two signatures. Thus every column it outputs is an actual relation in R_n; completeness of the enumeration is unnecessary.

For n=3, radii<=5 give a primitive rank15 relation lattice. `agent2_gl_closure.py` closes it under elementary GL3 actions and obtains primitive rank18 in Q of rank36. It verifies B kills it and rank(B)=18. Hence R_3=ker B.

For n=4, radius<=3 gives primitive rank102. GL4 closure has ranks190,206,206 in Q of rank276. Smith invariants all206 ones; exact rank(B)=70. Hence R_4=ker B.

`agent2_sparse_closure.py` embeds the rank4 relation basis on all four-element subsets and adds all pairs of Magnus automorphisms that commute (commuting is checked by exact free-word multiplication). It closes under elementary GL_n actions using integral unit-pivot elimination. Only reductions with pivot+/-1 are accepted; discarded candidates cannot invalidate the certificate. Every accepted row is therefore an actual group relation. Pivot ones imply its span is primitive. For n=5 it gets rank1040 and verifies Johnson rank185, sum1225=rank Q. For n=6 it gets rank3606 and verifies Johnson rank399, sum4005=rank Q. It directly checks B kills every source-relation basis row. Thus R_n=ker B for 3<=n<=6. For n=2 this follows from IA2=Inn(F2).

All files are self-contained except imports of our own helpers and SymPy. Runtime: n3 enumeration6sec; n3 closure<1sec; n4 enumeration/closure9sec; n5/6 sparse closure+verification2sec.

## General locality lemma: ker B is generated on at most six indices

Define the Morita trace t:T->S^2 H by embedding L3 in H^{tensor3}, contracting the dual root with the first tensor slot, and symmetrizing the remaining pair. For a root dual e_r^*,
 t(e_r^* tensor [[Xa,Xb],Xc])=delta(r,a) Xb Xc-delta(r,b) Xa Xc.
This formula shows directly tB=0. It also shows t is onto for n>=2: for XaXb choose r!=a and use e_r^* tensor [[Xr,Xa],Xb].

Take the usual integral Hall basis of L3, consisting of left-normed brackets [[Xi,Xj],Xk] with i!=j and the Hall order condition. Each basis vector of T has trace either0 or +/-one monomial XaXb. Therefore ker t has the following integral basis: every zero-trace Hall vector; and, for each monomial XaXb, signed differences between the Hall vectors tracing to that monomial and one chosen representative b0(a,b). Each such kernel-basis vector is supported on at most four indices (a,b and two possible root labels). Every representative b0(a,b) has support contained in {a,b,r0(a,b)}.

In ranks2,3,4 the bracket image equals ker t integrally. This follows by the exact sparse unit-pivot bracket-rank certificates (ranks1,18,70): the accepted primitive image sublattice has the same rational rank as ker t, which is saturated, so they agree. Thus each of the just-described ker t basis elements has a B-preimage using only the labels of that basis element, at most four. Choose one such preimage for each basis element. This gives an integral linear section s:ker t->Q.

Now take a basis vector c of Q, a wedge of two elementary derivations. It uses at most six labels. If Bc!=0, then one derivation's root must equal one of the other's leaves; hence c uses at most five distinct labels. B respects the Z^n weight (leaf counts minus root counts), so Bc is weight homogeneous.

If that weight has a negative coordinate, every Hall term of Bc has trace zero. The chosen s(Bc) is supported on labels already present in c.

If the weight is nonnegative, it is exactly the degree-two monomial XaXb. In the above ker t basis, expansion of Bc uses only Hall vectors already present in Bc together with the single chosen representative b0(a,b). Consequently s(Bc) is supported on the support of c together with the one additional root r0(a,b).

In either case, c-s(Bc) belongs to ker B and uses at most six labels. If Bc=0 the same conclusion holds by taking c itself. Finally, for x=sum_c a_c c in ker B,
 x=sum_c a_c(c-s(Bc)),
because s is linear and sum a_c Bc=0. This proves ker B is generated by the kernels restricted to sets of at most six labels.

Since those local kernels are actual source relations by the finite certificates, ker B=R_n for EVERY n>=2. Equivalently gamma_2(IA_n) intersection A_3(n)=gamma_3(IA_n); so (n,2) is excluded from D for every n.

## Required final small check
The sparse bracket routine already verifies primitive image ranks for n5,6. Run the same UnitLattice routine on bracket columns for n2,3,4 to explicitly print the primitive image ranks1,18,70 needed in the locality lemma. This is implemented in the next shared checker update.
```

```
def add(a,b,scale=1,p=1000003):

  if z:c[k]=z
  elif k in c:del c[k]

 out={}

   if i==x:
    key=w[:k]+v+w[k+1:]
    out[key]=(out.get(key,0)+t)%p
 return {k:v for k,v in out.items() if v}

def bracket(a,b,n,p):

   key=(i,t);out[key]=(out.get(key,0)+v*z)%p

import sys,pickle,time

from agent2_source_relations import generators,extend
from agent2_gl_closure import gl_action
from itertools import combinations
import sympy as S

class UnitLattice:
    def __init__(self):self.b={};self.queue=[];self.blocked=0
    def insert(self,v):
        v={i:int(c) for i,c in v.items() if c}
        while v:
            p=min(v);a=v[p]
            if p in self.b:
                w=self.b[p]
                for i,c in w.items():
                    t=v.get(i,0)-a*c
                    if t:v[i]=t
                    else:v.pop(i,None)
            else:
                if abs(a)!=1:self.blocked+=1;return False
                if a<0:v={i:-c for i,c in v.items()}
                self.b[p]=v;self.queue.append(v);return True
        return False

def action_maps(n):
    N=len(generators(n));P=list(combinations(range(N),2));pi={x:i for i,x in enumerate(P)}
    out=[]
    for p in range(n):
        for q in range(n):
            if p==q:continue
            A=S.eye(n);A[p,q]=1;T=gl_action(n,A)
            cols=[[(i,int(T[i,j])) for i in range(N) if T[i,j]] for j in range(N)]
            act=[]
            for i,j in P:
                v={}
                for a,x in cols[i]:
                    for b,y in cols[j]:
                        if a==b:continue
                        k=pi[min(a,b),max(a,b)];s=1 if a<b else -1
                        v[k]=v.get(k,0)+s*x*y
                act.append({k:c for k,c in v.items() if c})
            out.append(act)
    return out

def apply(act,v):
    ans={}
    for i,c in v.items():
        for j,d in act[i].items():
            t=ans.get(j,0)+c*d
            if t:ans[j]=t
            else:ans.pop(j,None)
    return ans

def seed(n,L):
    G=generators(n);gi={g:i for i,g in enumerate(G)};P=list(combinations(range(len(G)),2));pi={p:i for i,p in enumerate(P)}
    unit=tuple((i+1,) for i in range(n))
    for k,(i,j) in enumerate(P):
        if extend(extend(unit,G[i],1),G[j],1)==extend(extend(unit,G[j],1),G[i],1):L.insert({k:1})
    print('commuting rank',len(L.b),flush=True)

        for col in cols:L.insert({emb[i][0]:c*emb[i][1] for i,c in col.items()})
```

```
from pathlib import Path
code=r'''"""Exact integral derivation arithmetic; unit pivots certify primitive sublattices."""
from itertools import combinations

def generators(n):
 return [{(i,(a,b)):1,(i,(b,a)):-1} for i in range(n) for a,b in combinations(range(n),2)]
def apply(a,w):
 out={}
 for k,x in enumerate(w):
  for (i,v),t in a.items():
   if i==x:
    z=w[:k]+v+w[k+1:];out[z]=out.get(z,0)+t
 return {z:t for z,t in out.items() if t}
def bracket(a,b):
 out={}
 for A,B,s in [(a,b,1),(b,a,-1)]:
  for (i,w),v in B.items():
   for z,t in apply(A,w).items():
    key=(i,z);out[key]=out.get(key,0)+s*v*t
 return {k:v for k,v in out.items() if v}
class UnitLattice:
 def __init__(self):self.b={};self.blocked=0
 def insert(self,v):
  v=v.copy()
  while v:
   p=min(v);a=v[p]
   if p not in self.b:
    if abs(a)!=1:self.blocked+=1;return False
    self.b[p]={k:x*a for k,x in v.items()};return True
   for k,x in self.b[p].items():
    t=v.get(k,0)-a*x
    if t:v[k]=t
    else:v.pop(k,None)
  return False

def image(n,d):
 g=generators(n);bs=g
 for k in range(2,d+1):
  L=UnitLattice()
  for a in g:
   for b in bs:L.insert(bracket(a,b))
  bs=list(L.b.values())
  if k==2:expected=n*n*(n*n-1)//3-n*(n+1)//2
  elif k==3:expected=n*(n**4-n*n)//4-(n**3+2*n)//3
  else:expected=None
  if expected is not None:assert len(bs)==expected
  print('primitive integral image',n,k,len(bs),'blocked',L.blocked,flush=True)
 return bs
if __name__=='__main__':
 for n in range(2,7):image(n,3)
'''
```

```
from pathlib import Path
code=r'''"""Primitive integral degree<=3 Johnson-image certificate.
Primitivity: echelon max minor has only prime factor2; mod2 rank is full.
No source injectivity is claimed.
"""
from agent1_integral_derivations import generators,bracket,UnitLattice
from agent3_derivations import basis
from agent1_support_checks import lie,lyndon
from itertools import product

def necklace(w):return min(w[i:]+w[:i] for i in range(len(w)))
def check(n):
 gs=generators(n);bs=gs
 for d in [2,3]:
  L=UnitLattice()
  for a in gs:
   for b in bs:L.insert(bracket(a,b))
  bs=list(L.b.values());pivs=[v[p] for p,v in L.b.items()]
  assert all(x in [1,2] for x in pivs)
  mod2=len(basis(({k:x%2 for k,x in b.items() if x%2} for b in bs),2))
  assert mod2==len(bs)
  for b in bs:
   tr={}
   for (i,w),v in b.items():
    if w[0]==i:
     key=necklace(w[1:]);tr[key]=tr.get(key,0)+v
   assert all(x==0 for x in tr.values())
  expected=(n*n*(n*n-1)//3-n*(n+1)//2) if d==2 else n*(n**4-n*n)//4-(n**3+2*n)//3
  assert len(bs)==expected
  print('primitive integral image',n,d,len(bs),'minor2exponent',pivs.count(2),'mod2rank',mod2,flush=True)
 # Trace surjectivity, using integer gcd-echelon on cyclic target.
 T=UnitLattice()
 for w in product(range(n),repeat=4):
  if not lyndon(w):continue
  p=lie(w)
  for i in range(n):
   col={}
   for v,c in p.items():
    if v[0]==i:
     key=necklace(v[1:]);col[key]=col.get(key,0)+c
   T.insert({k:v for k,v in col.items() if v})
 assert len(T.b)==(n**3+2*n)//3
 assert all(v[p]==1 for p,v in T.b.items())
 print('cyclic trace is onto',n,len(T.b),flush=True)
if __name__=='__main__':
 for n in range(2,7):check(n)
'''
Path('workspace/agent1_degree3_image_checks.py').write_text(code)
import sys;sys.path.insert(0,'workspace')
exec(compile(code,'workspace/agent1_degree3_image_checks.py','exec'))
```

```
############################################################################
##
#M IA group
##
############################################################################
InstallMethod( EmbeddingOfIASubgroup, "for a free group automorphism group",
        [ IsAutomorphismGroupOfFreeGroup ],
        function(A)
# conventions on generators:
# M[i][j][k] is M_{x_i,[x_j,x_k]} if all variables i,j,k are <= n. x_i^-1 is represented as i+n.
# M[i][j][k] is a variable only if j<k. If j>k, it is stored as M[i][k][j]^-1.
# C[i][j] is C_{x_i,x_j} if i,j <= n. If j>n, then it is C_{x_i,x_{j-n}}^-1.
#
# Beware that GAP composes maps left-to-right, while the article composes maps right-to-left.
# GAP's commutator is [g,h] = g^-1h^-1gh, while the article's commutator is ghg^-1h^-1.
# Therefore, in converting an article formula to a GAP formula, multiplication order must be reversed,
# and commutators must be switched from [g,h] to [h,g].
#
    local n, F, G, C, M, i, p, q, r, s, t, u, v, rels, endos, Gendos, epi, alpha;
    
    G := Source(One(A)); # the free group
    n := RankOfFreeGroup(G);
    
    C := List(Arrangements([1..n],2),p->Concatenation("C(",String(p[1]),",",String(p[2]),")"));
    M := [];
    for p in Arrangements([1..n],3) do
        if p[2]>p[3] then continue; fi;
        Add(M,Concatenation("M(",String(p[1]),",[",String(p[2]),",",String(p[3]),"])"));
    od;
    F := FreeGroup(Concatenation(C,M));
    i := 1;
    epi := [];
    C := List([1..n],i->[]);
    for p in Arrangements([1..n],2) do
        C[p[1]][p[2]] := F.(i);
        C[p[1]][p[2]+n] := F.(i)^-1;
        q := ShallowCopy(GeneratorsOfGroup(G));
        q[p[1]] := q[p[2]]*q[p[1]]/q[p[2]];
        Add(epi,GroupHomomorphismByImages(G,q));
        i := i+1;
    od;
    M := List([1..2*n],i->List([1..2*n],j->[]));
    for p in Arrangements([1..n],3) do
        if p[2]>p[3] then continue; fi;
        M[p[1]][p[2]][p[3]] := F.(i);
        M[p[1]][p[3]][p[2]] := F.(i)^-1;
        q := ShallowCopy(GeneratorsOfGroup(G));
        q[p[1]] := Comm(q[p[2]]^-1,q[p[3]]^-1)*q[p[1]];
        Add(epi,GroupHomomorphismByImages(G,q));
        i := i+1;
            
        M[p[1]][p[3]][p[2]+n] := M[p[1]][p[2]][p[3]]^C[p[1]][p[2]];
        M[p[1]][p[2]][p[3]+n] := M[p[1]][p[3]][p[2]]^C[p[1]][p[3]];
            
        M[p[1]][p[3]+n][p[2]+n] := M[p[1]][p[2]][p[3]+n]^C[p[1]][p[2]];
        M[p[1]][p[2]+n][p[3]+n] := M[p[1]][p[3]][p[2]+n]^C[p[1]][p[3]];
            
        M[p[1]][p[3]+n][p[2]] := M[p[1]][p[2]][p[3]]^C[p[1]][p[3]];
        M[p[1]][p[2]+n][p[3]] := M[p[1]][p[3]][p[2]]^C[p[1]][p[2]];
        
        for s in [0,n] do for t in [0,n] do
            M[p[1]+n][p[2]+s][p[3]+t] := Comm(C[p[1]][p[2]+s]^-1,C[p[1]][p[3]+t]^-1) / M[p[1]][p[2]+s][p[3]+t];            
            M[p[1]+n][p[3]+s][p[2]+t] := Comm(C[p[1]][p[3]+s]^-1,C[p[1]][p[2]+t]^-1) / M[p[1]][p[3]+s][p[2]+t];            
        od; od;
    od;
    
    rels := [];
    
    # R0: M(x_a^alpha,[x_b^beta,x_c^gamma]) * M(x_a^alpha,[x_c^gamma,x_b^beta])
    # not necessary anymore, since we put only half the generators
    if true then
    for p in Arrangements([1..n],3) do
        for s in Tuples([0,n],3) do
            Add(rels,M[p[1]+s[1]][p[2]+s[2]][p[3]+s[3]]*M[p[1]+s[1]][p[3]+s[3]][p[2]+s[2]]);
        od;
    od;
    fi;
    
    # R1: [C(x_a,x_b),C(x_c,x_d)]
    for p in Arrangements([1..n],2) do
        if p<>[1,2] then continue; fi;
        for q in Arrangements([1..n],2) do
            if p[1]<>q[1] and p[1]<>q[2] and p[2]<>q[1] then
                Add(rels,Comm(C[p[1]][p[2]],C[q[1]][q[2]]));
            fi;
        od;
    od;
    
    # R2: [M(x_a^alpha,[x_b^beta,x_c^gamma]),M(x_d^delta,[x_e^epsilon,x_f^zeta])]
    for p in Arrangements([1..n],3) do for s in Tuples([0,n],3) do
        if p{[1,2]}<>[1,2] then continue; fi;
        for q in Arrangements([1..n],3) do for t in Tuples([0,n],3) do
            if q[2]>q[3] then continue; fi;
            if p[1]+s[1]<>q[1]+t[1] and not p[1] in q{[2,3]} and not q[1] in p{[2,3]} then

            fi;    
        od; od;
    od; od;

1,2] then continue; fi;

                Add(u,M[q[1]+s[1]][q[2]+s[2]][q[3]+s[3]]);
            fi;
        od; od;
        Add(endos,GroupHomomorphismByImages(F,t,u));
        u := ShallowCopy(GeneratorsOfGroup(G));
        v := ShallowCopy(GeneratorsOfGroup(G));
        if p[2]=0 then
            u[1] := (u[2]*u[1]^alpha)^alpha;
        else
            u[1] := (u[2]^-1*u[1]^alpha)^alpha;
        fi;
        Add(Gendos,GroupHomomorphismByImages(G,u));
    od;
    F := LPresentedGroup(F,[],endos,rels);
    F!.C := C; # hack: provide access to C and M generators as convenient tables
    F!.M := M;
    F!.Gendos := Gendos; # automorphisms of G such that endos[i] acts on Aut(G) by conjugation by Gendos[i]
    return GroupHomomorphismByImagesNC(F,A,epi);
end);
```

```
from pathlib import Path
p=r'''INTEGRAL IMAGE OF THE THIRD LOWER-CENTRAL JOHNSON HOMOMORPHISM
Let V_n=H_n^* tensor Lambda^2 H_n. Let J_3:L_3(V_n)->W_n=H_n^* tensor L_4(H_n) be evaluation as derivations. Let C_3(H_n)=(H_n^{tensor3})/(cyclic rotation differences), a free abelian group on necklaces of length3. Let Tr:W_n->C_3 be left contraction and cyclic projection. Then im J_3=ker Tr for every n>=2.

This is an IMAGE statement only; it DOES NOT prove the third graded Johnson map from gamma_3 IA/gamma_4 IA is injective.

Universal facts: Tr J_3=0, directly from the derivation bracket and cyclic projection. An element of L_3(V_n) is generated by [D,[E,F]]; the divergence identity gives Tr[D,[E,F]]=D Tr[E,F]-[E,F]Tr D=0 since Tr[E,F]=0 and Tr D is linear, so the second summand is the cyclic class of a free Lie word of degree3, which vanishes.

Trace preserves the weight (input-letter counts minus output index). For negative weights it is zero. A positive weight is a degree3 commutative monomial. If its support contains >=2 labels, choose an integral right inverse of the corresponding trace block using only those labels. Such a right inverse exists from the finite surjectivity checks in ranks2,3, since the full map decomposes by weight. For a cube a^3 choose an auxiliary root r!=a and use the rank2 right inverse, with r chosen as1 unless a=1, in which case r=2. Thus we have an integral right inverse q:C_3->W_n, local on <=3 labels, except a cube requiring one auxiliary root.

For each coordinate Lyndon basis column w of W_n, w-qTr(w) belongs to ker Tr. These columns generate ker Tr. A zero-trace w is supported on at most5 labels. A nonzero-trace w has its root among its input letters, hence is supported on at most4 labels; qTr(w) adds at most one root in the cube case. Hence every generator of ker Tr is supported on at most5 labels.

Exact finite certificates in agent1_degree3_image_checks.py prove im J_3=ker Tr in ranks2..5: image ranks2,43,216,705, trace ranks4,11,24,45, and W ranks6,54,240,750. Trace is onto with primitive unit-echelon basis. The image lattice is primitive in the ambient free associative tensor module: its integer row-echelon pivots are only1 or2, yielding a maximal minor that is a power of2; its reduction modulo2 has full rational rank. Thus no prime can divide its Smith torsion. This avoids an unmanageable full dense Smith calculation.

Embedding the local image preimages of the above <=5-label generators proves ker Tr subset im J_3 for all n, completing the proof. The script verifies traces of all integral degree3 image basis vectors vanish; the universal divergence calculation replaces finite tests for that identity.
'''
Path('workspace/agent1_degree3_image_proof.txt').write_text(p)
print(p)

```

```
--- stdout ---
############################################################################
##
#W nq.gi			LPRES				René Hartung
##

############################################################################
##
#M  NilpotentQuotient ( <LpGroup>, <int> ) . . for invariant LpGroups
##
## computes a weighted nilpotent presentation for the class-<int> quotient
## of the invariant <LpGroup> if it already has a nilpotent quotient system.
##
InstallOtherMethod( NilpotentQuotient,
  "for invariantly L-presented groups (with qs) and positive integer",
  true,
  [ IsLpGroup and HasIsInvariantLPresentation and IsInvariantLPresentation
    and HasNilpotentQuotientSystem, IsPosInt], 0,
  function(G,c)
  local Q, 	# current quotient system
        QS,	# old quotient system
	H,	# the nilpotent quotient of <G>
      	i,	# loop variable
	time,	# runtime
        j;	# nilpotency class

  # known quotient system of <G>
  Q:=NilpotentQuotientSystem(G);
 
  # nilpotency class
  j:=MaximumList(Q.Weights,0);
 
  if c=j then 
    # the given nilpotency class <j> is already known
    H:=PcpGroupByCollectorNC(Q.Pccol);
    SetLowerCentralSeriesOfGroup(H,LPRES_LCS(Q));

    return(H);
  elif c<j then
    # the given nilpotency class <c> is already computed 
    QS:=SmallerQuotientSystem(Q,c);

    # build the nilpotent quotient with lcs
    H:=PcpGroupByCollectorNC(QS.Pccol);
    SetLowerCentralSeriesOfGroup(H,LPRES_LCS(QS));

    return(H);
  else
    if HasLargestNilpotentQuotient(G) then 
      return(LargestNilpotentQuotient(G));
    fi;

    # extend the largest known quotient system
    for i in [j+1..c] do
      QS:=ShallowCopy(Q);

      time := Runtime();

      # extend the quotient system of G/\gamma_i to G/\gamma_{i+1}
      Q:=ExtendQuotientSystem(Q);
      if Q = fail then 
        return(fail);
      fi;

      # if we couldn't extend the quotient system any more, we're finished
      if QS.Weights=Q.Weights then 
        SetLargestNilpotentQuotient(G,PcpGroupByCollectorNC(Q.Pccol));
        break;
      fi;

      if Length(Q.Weights)-Length(QS.Weights) > InfoLPRES_MAX_GENS then 
        Info(InfoLPRES,1,"Class ",Maximum(Q.Weights),": ",
	               Length(Q.Weights)-Length(QS.Weights), " generators");
      else
        Info(InfoLPRES,1,"Class ",Maximum(Q.Weights),": ",
	               Length(Q.Weights)-Length(QS.Weights),
		       " generators with relative orders: ",
		       RelativeOrders(Q.Pccol)
		       {[Length(QS.Weights)+1..Length(Q.Weights)]});
      fi;
      Info(InfoLPRES,2,"Runtime for this step ",StringTime(Runtime()-time) );
    od;

    # store the largest nilpotent quotient system
    ResetFilterObj(G,NilpotentQuotientSystem);
    SetNilpotentQuotientSystem(G,Q);

    # build the nilpotent quotient with its lower central series attribute
    H:=PcpGroupByCollectorNC(Q.Pccol);
    SetLowerCentralSeriesOfGroup(H,LPRES_LCS(Q));

    return(H);
  fi;
  end);

############################################################################
##
#M  NilpotentQuotient ( <LpGroup>, <int> ) . . for invariant LpGroups 
##
## computes a weighted nilpotent presentation for the class-<int> quotient
## of the invariant <LpGroup>.
##
InstallOtherMethod( NilpotentQuotient,
  "for an invariantly L-presented group and a positive integer",
  true,
  [ IsLpGroup and HasIsInvariantLPresentation and IsInvariantLPresentation,
    IsPosInt ], 0,
  function(G,c)
  local Q,	# current quotient system
	H,	# the nilpotent quotient of <G>
	QS,	# old quotient system
	i,	# loop variable
	time;	# runtime

  time:=Runtime();
  # Compute a confluent nilpotent presentation for G/G'
  Q:=InitQuotientSystem(G);

  if Length(Q.Weights) > InfoLPRES_MAX_GENS then
    Info(InfoLPRES,1,"Class ",1,": ",Length(Q.Weights), " generators");
  else
    Info(InfoLPRES,1,"Class ",1,": ",Length(Q.Weights),
		     " generators with relative orders: ",
		     RelativeOrders(Q.Pccol));
  fi;
  Info(InfoLPRES,2,"Runtime for this step ", StringTime(Runtime()-time));
  
  for i in [1..c-1] do 
    # copy the old quotient system to compare with the extended qs.

  ResetFilterObj(G,NilpotentQuotientSystem);

        if Length(Q.Weights)-Length(QS.Weights) > InfoLPRES_MAX_GENS then 
          Info(InfoLPRES,1,"Class ",Maximum(Q.Weights),": ",
 			 Length(Q.Weights)-Length(QS.Weights), " generators");
        else
          Info(InfoLPRES,1,"Class ",Maximum(Q.Weights),": ",
			 Length(Q.Weights)-Length(QS.Weights),
			 " generators with relative orders: ",
			 RelativeOrders(Q.Pccol)
			   {[Length(QS.Weights)+1..Length(Q.Weights)]});
        fi;
      fi;
      Info(InfoLPRES,2,"Runtime for this step ", StringTime(Runtime()-time));
  
    od;

    # store the largest nilpotent quotient system
    ResetFilterObj(G,NilpotentQuotientSystem);
    SetNilpotentQuotientSystem(G,Q);

    return(Q.Epimorphism); 
  fi;
  end);

############################################################################
##
#M  NqEpimorphismNilpotentQuotient ( <LpGroup>, <int> )
##
## computes an epimorphism from <LpGroup> onto its class-<int> quotient.
##
InstallOtherMethod( NqEpimorphismNilpotentQuotient,
  "for an invariantly L-presented group",
  true,
  [ IsLpGroup and HasIsInvariantLPresentation and IsInvariantLPresentation,
    IsPosInt], 0,
  function(G,c)
  local H; 	# nilpotent quotient <G>/gamma_<c>(<G>)

  # compute the nilpotent quotient of <G>
  H:=NilpotentQuotient(G,c);
 
  return(NilpotentQuotientSystem(G).Epimorphism);
  end);

############################################################################
##
#M  NqEpimorphismNilpotentQuotient ( <LpGroup> , <PcpGroup> ) 
##
## computes an epimorphism from the invariant LpGroup into the nilpotent 
## quotient <PcpGroup>.
##
InstallOtherMethod( NqEpimorphismNilpotentQuotient,
  "for an L-presented group and its nilpotent quotient",
  true,  
  [ IsLpGroup and HasIsInvariantLPresentation and IsInvariantLPresentation
    and HasNilpotentQuotientSystem, IsPcpGroup ], 0,
  function(G,H)
  local ftl,	# collector of the PcpGroup <H>
	Q,	# largest known nilpotent quotient system of G
     	QS, 	# nilpotent quotient system of <H>
   	imgs,	# images of the epimorphism
        i,	# loop variable
        c,	# nilpotency class of <H>
	n;	# number of generators of G/gamma_c(G)

  # number of generators of the Pcp group (used to determine its qs.)
  ftl:=Collector(H);
  n:=NumberOfGenerators(ftl);

  # the largest known quotient system
  Q:=NilpotentQuotientSystem(G);
  
  # nilpotency class of <H>
  c:=Q.Weights[n];

  # determine the quotient system of <H>
  QS:=SmallerQuotientSystem(Q,c);
 
  imgs:=[];
  for i in [1..Length(QS.Imgs)] do
    if IsInt(QS.Imgs[i]) then 
      imgs[i]:=[QS.Imgs[i],1];
    else
      imgs[i]:=QS.Imgs[i];
    fi;
  od;
  imgs:=List(imgs,x->PcpElementByGenExpList(ftl,x));
  return(GroupHomomorphismByImagesNC(G,H,GeneratorsOfGroup(G),imgs));
  end);

############################################################################
##
#F  LPRES_LCS( <QS> )
##
## computes the lower central series of a nilpotent quotient given by a 
## quotient system <QS>.
##
InstallGlobalFunction( LPRES_LCS,
  function(Q)
  local weights,	# weights-function of <Q>
  	i,		# loop variable
  	c, 		# nilpotency class of <Q>
 	H,		# nilpotent presentation corr. to <Q>
  	gens,		# generators of <H>
 	lcs;		# lower central series of <H>

  # nilpotent presentation group corr. to <Q>
  H:=PcpGroupByCollectorNC(Q.Pccol);

  # generators of <H>
  gens:=GeneratorsOfGroup(H);

  # weights function of the given quotient system
  weights:=Q.Weights;

  # nilpotency class of <Q>
  c:=MaximumList(weights,0);

  # build the lower central series
  lcs:=[];
  lcs[c+1]:=SubgroupByIgs(H,[]);
  lcs[1]:=H;
 
  for i in [2..c] do
    # the lower central series as subgroups by an induced generating system
    # with weights at least <i>
    lcs[i]:=SubgroupByIgs(H,gens{Filtered([1..Length(gens)],x->weights[x]>=i)});
  od;

  return(lcs);
  end);

############################################################################
##
#A  LargestNilpotentQuotient( <LpGroup> )
##
InstallMethod( LargestNilpotentQuotient,
  "for an L-presented group",
  true,
  [ IsLpGroup ], 0,
  NilpotentQuotient);

############################################################################
##
#A  NilpotentQuotients( <LpGroup> ) .  .  .  .  . for invariant LpGroups
##
InstallMethod( NilpotentQuotients,
  "for an invariantly L-presented group with a quotient system",
  true,
  [ IsLpGroup and HasNilpotentQuotientSystem ], 0,
  function ( G )
  local c;	# nilpotency class of the known quotient system
   
  c:=MaximumList(NilpotentQuotientSystem(G).Weights,0); 
  return( List([1..c], i -> NqEpimorphismNilpotentQuotient(G,i) ) );
  end);

############################################################################
##
#M  NqEpimorphismNilpotentQuotient( <FpGroup> )
##
InstallOtherMethod( NqEpimorphismNilpotentQuotient,
  "for an FpGroup using the LPRES-package", true,
  [ IsFpGroup, IsPosInt ], 0,
  function( G, c )
  local iso, 	# isomorphism from FpGroup to LpGroup
	mapi,	# MappingGeneratorsImages of <iso>
	epi;	# epimorphism from LpGroup onto its nilpotent quotient

  iso  := IsomorphismLpGroup( G );
  mapi := MappingGeneratorsImages( iso );
  epi  := NqEpimorphismNilpotentQuotient( Range( iso ), c );
  return( GroupHomomorphismByImages( G, Range( epi ), mapi[1], 
                        List( mapi[1], x -> Image( epi, Image( iso, x ) ) ) ) );
  end);
```

```
import json,sys,pickle,time
sys.path.insert(0,'workspace')
from agent2_cubic_source import magnus3,IntegralLattice
from agent2_sparse_closure import apply
from itertools import product
import sympy as S
from sympy.matrices.normalforms import smith_normal_form
from sympy.polys.domains import ZZ
from collections import Counter
N=9;off=81;data=json.load(open('workspace/agent4_gap_ia3_data.json'))
def vector(p):
    v={}
    for u,c in p.items():
        if len(u)==2:v[u[0]*N+u[1]]=c
        if len(u)==3:v[off+(u[0]*N+u[1])*N+u[2]]=c
    return v

def action(ws):
    ps=[magnus3(tuple(w[::-1]),N) for w in ws]
    lin=[{u:c for u,c in p.items() if len(u)==1} for p in ps]
    quad=[{u:c for u,c in p.items() if len(u)==2} for p in ps]
    act=[]
    for i,j in product(range(N),repeat=2):
        p={}
        for P,Q in [(lin[i],lin[j]),(quad[i],lin[j]),(lin[i],quad[j])]:
            for x,c in P.items():
                for y,d in Q.items():p[x+y]=p.get(x+y,0)+c*d
        act.append(vector(p))
    for i,j,k in product(range(N),repeat=3):
        p={}
        for x,c in lin[i].items():
            for y,d in lin[j].items():
                for z,e in lin[k].items():p[x+y+z]=p.get(x+y+z,0)+c*d*e
        act.append(vector(p))
    return act

def comm(v,k):
    ans={}
    for i,c in v.items():
        if i>=off:continue
        a,b=divmod(i,N);j=off+(a*N+b)*N+k;l=off+(k*N+a)*N+b
        ans[j]=ans.get(j,0)+c;ans[l]=ans.get(l,0)-c
    return ans
start=time.time();L=IntegralLattice()
for r in data['rels']:
    p=magnus3(tuple(r['word'][::-1]),N)
    assert not any(len(u)==1 for u in p)
    L.insert(vector(p))
acts=[action(e['images']) for e in data['endomorphisms']];idx=0
while idx<len(L.queue):
    v=L.queue[idx];idx+=1
    for k in range(N):L.insert(comm(v,k))
    for act in acts:L.insert(apply(act,v))
for iteration in range(10):
    changed=0
    for v in list(L.b.values()):
        for k in range(N):changed+=L.insert(comm(v,k))
        for act in acts:changed+=L.insert(apply(act,v))
    print('stability',iteration,'changes',changed,flush=True)
    if not changed:break
C={p-off:{i-off:c for i,c in v.items()} for p,v in L.b.items() if p>=off}
M=S.zeros(729,len(C))
for j,v in enumerate(C.values()):
    for i,c in v.items():M[i,j]=c
D=smith_normal_form(M,domain=ZZ);ds=[abs(D[i,i]) for i in range(len(C))]
print('Direct LPRES mixed rank',len(L.b),'pure cubic rank',len(C),'SNF',dict(Counter(ds)),'secs',time.time()-start,flush=True)
pickle.dump(C,open('workspace/agent2_direct_lpres_cubic.pkl','wb'))
```

```
import sys,pickle,time
sys.path.insert(0,'workspace')
from agent2_source_relations import generators,extend,inv
from agent2_sparse_closure import UnitLattice
from agent2_gl_closure import gl_action
from itertools import combinations,product
import sympy as S

class IntegralLattice:
    def __init__(self):self.b={};self.queue=[];self.blocked=0
    def insert(self,v):
        v={i:int(c) for i,c in v.items() if c}
        changed=False
        while v:
            p=min(v);a=v[p]
            if p not in self.b:
                if a<0:v={i:-c for i,c in v.items()}
                self.b[p]=v;self.queue.append(v);return True
            w=self.b[p];d=w[p];q=a//d
            for i,c in w.items():
                t=v.get(i,0)-q*c
                if t:v[i]=t
                else:v.pop(i,None)
            if v.get(p,0):
                # Euclidean exchange: preserve old pivot row as next remainder.
                self.b[p]=v;self.queue.append(v);v=w;changed=True
        return changed

def magnus3(word,N):
    p={():1}
    for x in word:
        a=abs(x)-1; terms=[((),1),((a,),1)] if x>0 else [(tuple([a]*k),(-1)**k) for k in range(4)]
        q={}
        for u,c in p.items():
            for v,d in terms:
                if len(u)+len(v)<=3:q[u+v]=q.get(u+v,0)+c*d
        p={u:c for u,c in q.items() if c}
    return p

def compose(A,B):
    from agent2_source_relations import cat
    Ai=[inv(w) for w in A]
    return tuple(cat(*(A[x-1] if x>0 else Ai[-x-1] for x in w)) for w in B)

def cubic_sources(depth=4):
    n=3;G=generators(n);N=len(G);off=N*N
    unit=tuple((i+1,) for i in range(n));seen={unit:()};layer=[(unit,())];L=IntegralLattice(); count=0
    from agent2_source_relations import cat
    targets={};found={}
    for a in range(n):
        for b in range(n):
            if a==b:continue
            U=list(unit);Ui=list(unit);U[b]=cat(unit[b],unit[a]);Ui[b]=cat(unit[b],inv(unit[a]));U=tuple(U);Ui=tuple(Ui)
            for k,g in enumerate(G):
                target=compose(compose(U,extend(unit,g,1)),Ui)
                targets.setdefault(target,[]).append((a,b,k))
    for d in range(1,depth+1):
        nxt=[]
        for A,w in layer:
            for a,g in enumerate(G):
                for s in (1,-1):
                    letter=s*(a+1)
                    if w and w[-1]==-letter:continue
                    B=extend(A,g,s);ww=w+(letter,)
                    if B not in seen:
                        seen[B]=ww;nxt.append((B,ww))
                        if B in targets:
                            for label in targets[B]:found[label]=ww
                        continue
                    w0=seen[B]
                    if ww==w0:continue
                    p=magnus3(ww+inv(w0),N)
                    assert not any(len(u)==1 for u in p)
                    v={}
                    for u,c in p.items():
                        if len(u)==2:v[u[0]*N+u[1]]=c
                        if len(u)==3:v[off+(u[0]*N+u[1])*N+u[2]]=c
                    L.insert(v);count+=1
        layer=nxt
        print('depth',d,'seen',len(seen),'relations tested',count,'lattice rank',len(L.b),'pure cubic',sum(p>=off for p in L.b),'blocked',L.blocked,flush=True)
    print('Found Nielsen conjugate lifts',len(found),'of54; lengths',sorted(set(map(len,found.values()))),flush=True)
    pickle.dump(found,open('workspace/agent2_nielsen_lifts.pkl','wb'))
    pickle.dump(L.b,open('workspace/agent2_ia3_mixed_relations.pkl','wb'))
    C=IntegralLattice()
    for p,v in L.b.items():
        if p>=off:C.insert({i-off:c for i,c in v.items()})
    # Full known quadratic relation lattice gives valid cubic commutator rels.
    H=pickle.load(open('workspace/agent2_ia3_GL_relations.pkl','rb'));P=list(combinations(range(N),2))
    for col in range(H.cols):
        p={}
        for r,(i,j) in enumerate(P):
            c=int(H[r,col])
            if c:p[(i,j)]=c;p[(j,i)]=-c
        for k in range(N):
            v={}
            for (i,j),c in p.items():
                a=(i*N+j)*N+k;b=(k*N+i)*N+j
                v[a]=v.get(a,0)+c;v[b]=v.get(b,0)-c
            C.insert(v)
    print('cubic plus quadratic consequences',len(C.b),'blocked',C.blocked,flush=True)
    return C

def tensor_actions(n=3):
    N=len(generators(n));out=[]
    for a in range(n):
        for b in range(n):
            if a==b:continue
            A=S.eye(n);A[a,b]=1;T=gl_action(n,A);col=[[(i,int(T[i,j])) for i in range(N) if T[i,j]] for j in range(N)]
            act=[]
            for i,j,k in product(range(N),repeat=3):
                v={}
                for x,c in col[i]:
                    for y,d in col[j]:
                        for z,e in col[k]:v[(x*N+y)*N+z]=c*d*e
                act.append(v)
            out.append(act)
    # Include determinant-minus-one inversion, not merely SL3.
    A=S.eye(n);A[0,0]=-1;T=gl_action(n,A)
    diag=[int(T[i,i]) for i in range(N)]
    out.append([{(i*N+j)*N+k:diag[i]*diag[j]*diag[k]} for i,j,k in product(range(N),repeat=3)])
    return out

def run():
    start=time.time();C=cubic_sources(depth=5);actions=tensor_actions();idx=0
    from agent2_sparse_closure import apply
    while idx<len(C.queue):
        v=C.queue[idx];idx+=1
        for act in actions:C.insert(apply(act,v))
    print('FINAL CUBIC RELATION RANK',len(C.b),'pivot values',sorted(set(v[p] for p,v in C.b.items())),'blocked',C.blocked,'secs',time.time()-start,flush=True)
    from sympy.matrices.normalforms import smith_normal_form
    from sympy.polys.domains import ZZ
    M=S.zeros(729,len(C.b))
    for j,v in enumerate(C.b.values()):
        for i,c in v.items():M[i,j]=c
    D=smith_normal_form(M,domain=ZZ)
    diag=[abs(D[i,i]) for i in range(len(C.b))]
    print('Smith invariant values',sorted(set(diag)),flush=True)
    from collections import Counter
    print('Smith counts',dict(Counter(diag)),flush=True)
    pickle.dump(C.b,open('workspace/agent2_ia3_cubic_relations.pkl','wb'))
    return C
if __name__=='__main__':run()
```

```
from pathlib import Path
text=r'''CENTRALIZER REFORMULATION OF THE REQUESTED SET
For n>=2 let G=IA_n and L=gr^gamma G. There is an injective graded Lie map i:Lie(H_n)->L induced by inner automorphisms. Its image J is a graded Lie ideal. The homogeneous Johnson kernel K_c=ker(L_c->Hom(H_n,Lie_{c+1}(H_n))) equals C_L(J)_c, the degree-c part of the centralizer of J. Hence D={(n,c):C_L(J)_c!=0}.

Proof. Standard commutator calculus gives [A_r,A_s] subset A_{r+s}, hence gamma_r G subset A_r. For all n>=2, an inner automorphism inn_w belongs to A_r iff the image of w is central in F_n/gamma_{r+1}, iff w belongs to gamma_r F_n. (The center of a free nilpotent group of rank>=2 is its final lower-central term.) If inn_w for w in gamma_r F_n belongs to gamma_{r+1}G, it lies in A_{r+1}, so w lies in gamma_{r+1}F_n. This proves injection on the graded groups. Inn(F_n) normal in G proves J is an ideal.

The exact identity [alpha,inn_x]=inn_{alpha(x)x^{-1}} holds with the commutator convention of the question. Thus for alpha in gamma_c G and x a free generator, its bracket with i(X) is i(tau_c(alpha)(X)). Since i is injective, alpha's degree-c class centralizes i(H_n) iff tau_c(alpha)=0. The ideal J is generated as a Lie algebra by i(H_n), so by Jacobi, centralizing i(H_n) is equivalent to centralizing all J. This proves the equality.

Consequences. K is a graded Lie ideal and K intersect J=0 (the center of a free Lie algebra of rank>=2 iszero). If the center of L were known to be zero, nonzero K_c would imply nonzero K_{c+1}, because L is generated by degree1: a nonzero K_c element not central must have a nonzero bracket with some degree1 generator. But proving center(L)=0 cannot be replaced by center(G)=0, and is not supplied here. Thus upward propagation of D needs a separate source-side theorem.

The Johnson target is free abelian, so torsion in L_c gives a nonzero element of K_c. Conversely it is NOT proved that all K_c is torsion; that would be a rational Andreadakis assertion.
'''
Path('workspace/agent1_centralizer_reduction.txt').write_text(text)
print(text)

```

```
Proof. Standard commutator calculus gives [A_r,A_s] subset A_{r+s}, hence gamma_r G subset A_r. For all n>=2, an inner automorphism inn_w belongs to A_r iff the image of w is central in F_n/gamma_{r+1}, iff w belongs to gamma_r F_n. (The center of a free nilpotent group of rank>=2 is its final lower-central term.) If inn_w for w in gamma_r F_n belongs to gamma_{r+1}G, it lies in A_{r+1}, so w lies in gamma_{r+1}F_n. This proves injection on the graded groups. Inn(F_n) normal in G proves J is an ideal.

The exact identity [alpha,inn_x]=inn_{alpha(x)x^{-1}} holds with the commutator convention of the question. Thus for alpha in gamma_c G and x a free generator, its bracket with i(X) is i(tau_c(alpha)(X)). Since i is injective, alpha's degree-c class centralizes i(H_n) iff tau_c(alpha)=0. The ideal J is generated as a Lie algebra by i(H_n), so by Jacobi, centralizing i(H_n) is equivalent to centralizing all J. This proves the equality.

Consequences. K is a graded Lie ideal and K intersect J=0 (the center of a free Lie algebra of rank>=2 iszero). If the center of L were known to be zero, nonzero K_c would imply nonzero K_{c+1}, because L is generated by degree1: a nonzero K_c element not central must have a nonzero bracket with some degree1 generator. But proving center(L)=0 cannot be replaced by center(G)=0, and is not supplied here. Thus upward propagation of D needs a separate source-side theorem.

The Johnson target is free abelian, so torsion in L_c gives a nonzero element of K_c. Conversely it is NOT proved that all K_c is torsion; that would be a rational Andreadakis assertion.
```

```
from itertools import combinations
from collections import Counter
from math import gcd
import sympy as S
from sympy.matrices.normalforms import smith_normal_form, hermite_normal_form
from sympy.polys.domains import ZZ

def red(w):
    st=[]
    for x in w:
        if st and st[-1]==-x:st.pop()
        else:st.append(x)
    return tuple(st)
def inv(w):return tuple(-i for i in w[::-1])
def cat(*words):return red(x for w in words for x in w)
def cm(a,b):return cat(a,b,inv(a),inv(b))
def generators(n):
    out=[]
    for i in range(n):
        for j in range(n):
            if i!=j:out.append(('C',i,j))
        for j,k in combinations(range(n),2):
            if i not in (j,k):out.append(('M',i,j,k))
    return out

def extend(A,g,s):
    typ,i,*args=g
    B=list(A)
    if typ=='C':
        j=args[0]; v=A[j] if s==1 else inv(A[j]);B[i]=cat(v,A[i],inv(v))
    else:
        j,k=args;v=cm(A[j],A[k]);B[i]=cat(A[i],v if s==1 else inv(v))
    return tuple(B)

def lattice_relations(n=3,depth=4):
    G=generators(n);N=len(G)
    pairs=list(combinations(range(N),2));pidx={p:i for i,p in enumerate(pairs)};M=len(pairs)
    unit=tuple((i+1,) for i in range(n))
    z=(0,)*N; zz=(0,)*M
    seen={unit:(z,zz,())}; layer=[(unit,z,zz,())]
    rels={}; found=Counter()
    for d in range(1,depth+1):
        nxt=[]
        for A,e,c,w in layer:
            for a,g in enumerate(G):
                for s in (1,-1):
                    letter=s*(a+1)
                    if w and w[-1]==-letter:continue
                    B=extend(A,g,s)
                    f=list(e); f[a]+=s; f=tuple(f)
                    v=list(c)
                    for j in range(a+1,N):v[pidx[a,j]]-=s*e[j]
                    v=tuple(v); ww=w+(letter,)
                    if B in seen:
                        f0,v0,w0=seen[B]
                        assert f0==f
                        rel=tuple(x-y for x,y in zip(v,v0))
                        if any(rel):
                            if next(x for x in rel if x)<0:rel=tuple(-x for x in rel); ww,w0=w0,ww
                            if rel not in rels:rels[rel]=(ww,w0);found[d]+=1
                    else:
                        seen[B]=(f,v,ww);nxt.append((B,f,v,ww))
        layer=nxt
        R=S.Matrix(list(rels)).T if rels else S.zeros(M,0)
        print('depth',d,'seen',len(seen),'layer',len(layer),'relations',len(rels),'rank',R.rank(),flush=True)
    R=S.Matrix(list(rels)).T
    H=hermite_normal_form(R)
    D=smith_normal_form(H,domain=ZZ)
    elementary=[D[i,i] for i in range(min(D.shape))]
    print('relation rank',H.cols,'Smith invariants',elementary,flush=True)
    return G,pairs,rels,H

if __name__=='__main__':
    import pickle
    G,pairs,rels,H=lattice_relations(depth=5)
    with open('workspace/agent2_ia3_quadratic_relations.pkl','wb') as f:pickle.dump((G,pairs,rels,H),f)

def reduce(w):
 out=[]
 for i in w:

 return tuple(out)
def substitute(w,aut):
 return reduce(j for i in w for j in (aut[i-1] if i>0 else inv(aut[-i-1])))
def compose(a,b):return tuple(substitute(w,a) for w in b)
```

```
IsomorphismFpGroup( <group> )
##
##  returns an isomorphism from an automorphism group of a free group
##  to a finitely presented group.
##
##  The presentation follows Bernhard Neumann (see ../doc/manual.bib)
##  Numbers in the comments refer to the equation numbers in that paper.
##
InstallMethod( IsomorphismFpGroup,
    "for automorphism groups of free groups",
    [ IsAutomorphismGroupOfFreeGroup ],
    function( aut )

    local n, f, fp, O, P, U, S, T, Q, R, rels, moreRels,
                    o, p, u, s, t, q, r, iso, isoinv;

    n := RankOfFreeGroup( AutomorphismDomain ( aut ));

    if n = 0 then

        fp := FreeGroup( 0 );

        iso := aut -> One( fp );

    elif n = 1 then

        f := FreeGroup( "O" );
        O := f.1;
        rels := [ O^2 ];  # 6a
        fp := f / rels;

        o := fp.1;
        iso := aut -> o ^ (Order(aut) - 1);

    elif n = 2 then

        f := FreeGroup( "O", "P", "U" );
        O := f.1;  P := f.2;  U := f.3;
        rels := [ P^2                # 5a
                , O^2                # 6a
                , (O*P)^4            # 8a
                , Comm( U, O*U*O )   # 7e
                , (P*O*P*U)^2        # 7i
                , (U*P*O)^3          # 8b
                ];
        fp := f / rels;

        o := fp.1;  p := fp.2;  u := fp.3;
        iso := FGA_CurryAutToPQOU( p, p, o, u); # Q=P

    elif n = 3 then

        f := FreeGroup( "S", "T", "U" );
        S := f.1;  T := f.2;  U := f.3;
        rels := [ (S^5*T^-1)^2                     # 19a
                , T^-1*S*T^2*S^8*T^-1*S*T^2*S^-4   # 19b
                , (S^4*T^-1*S*T^-1)^2              # 19c
                , T^4                              # 19d
                , Comm( U, S^2*T^-1*S*T^-1*S^2 )   # 19e
                , Comm( U, S^-2*T^-1*S*T^-1*U*S^-2*T^-1*S*T^-1) # 19f
        # wrong:  Comm( U, S^2*T^-1*S*T*S*T^-1*U*T*S^-1*T^-1*S^-1*T*S^2 ) # 19g
                , Comm( U, S^-2*T^-1*S*T*S*T^-1*U*
                              T*S^-1*T^-1*S^-1*T*S^2 ) # 19g corrected
                , Comm( U, T^-1*S*T^2*U*T^-1*S*T^2 )   # 19h
                , S^-2*T^-1*S*T^2*S^2*U*S^2*T^-1*S*T^2*S^2*
                  U*S^-2*U*S^2*U^-1*S^-2*U^-1               # 19i
                , (S^-2*T^-1*S*T*U)^2              # 19j
                , (U*T)^3                          # 19k
                ];
        fp := f / rels;

        s := fp.1;  t := fp.2;  u := fp.3;
        iso := FGA_CurryAutToPQOU( t*s^3*(s*t^-1)^2  # 16c
                                 , s^4               # 16a
                                 , s^3*(s*t^-1)^2    # 16b
                                 , u
                                 );

    elif IsEvenInt(n) then

        f := FreeGroup( "Q", "R" );
        Q := f.1;  R := f.2;
        rels := [ (R^3*(Q*R^3)^(n-1))^2            # 22a
                , (Q*R^3)^(2*(n-1))                # 22d
                , Q^n                              # 22e
                , Comm( (Q*R^3)^(n-1),
                        Q^-1*R^3*(Q*R^3)^(n-1)*Q ) # 22f
                , Comm( Q^-2*R^4*Q^2*R^-3,
                        Q*R^-3*Q^-1*R^-3*Q )       # 22h
                , Comm( R^4, (Q*R^3)^(n-1) )       # 22j
                , Comm( R^4, Q^-2*R^4*Q^2 )        # 22k
                , Comm( Q^-2*R^4*Q^2*R^-3,
                        (Q*R^3)^(n-1)*Q^-2*R^4*Q^2*R^-3*(Q*R^3)^(n-1) )  # 22l
                , Comm( Q^-2*R^4*Q^2*R^-3,
                        R^-3*Q^-1*Q^-2*R^4*Q^2*R^-3*Q*R^3 )  # 22m
                , Comm( Q^-2*R^4*Q^2*R^-3,
                        R^-3*Q^-1*R^-3*(Q*R^3)^n*Q^-2*R^4*
                          Q^2*R^-3*(Q*R^3)^-n*R^3*Q*R^3   )  # 22n
                , (Q*R^3)^-n*Q^-2*R^4*Q^2*R^-3*(Q*R^3)^n*
                    Q^-3*R^4*Q^2*R^-3*Q^-1*R^4*Q^2*R^-3*
                    Q^-1*R^3*Q^-2*R^-4*Q^3*R^3*Q^-2*R^-4*Q^2 # 22o
                , (R^3*(Q*R^3)^(n-1)*Q^-2*R^4*Q^2)^2         # 22p
                , R^12                                       # 22q
                ];
        # finally the relations 22c:
        moreRels := List( [ 2 .. n/2 ],
                          i -> Comm( R^3*(Q*R^3)^(n-1),
                                     Q^-i*R^3*(Q*R^3)^(n-1)*Q^i ) );
        fp := f / Concatenation(rels, moreRels);

        q := fp.1;  r := fp.2;
        iso := FGA_CurryAutToPQOU( r^3*(q*r^3)^(n-1)   # 21b
                                 , q
                                 , (q*r^3)^(n-1)       # 21a
                                 , q^-2*r^4*q^2*r^-3   # 21c
                                 );

    else  #  n > 3, odd

        f := FreeGroup( "S", "R" );
        S := f.1;  R := f.2;
        rels := [ (R^3*S^n*(S*R^-3)^(n-1))^2                 # 25a
                , (S^(n+1)*R^3*S^n*(S*R^-3)^(n-1))^(n-1)*
                    S^((-n)*(n-1))                           # 25b
                , ((S^n)*(S*R^-3)^(n-1))^2                   # 25d
                , Comm( S^n*(S*R^-3)^(n-1),
                        S^-(n+1)*R^3*S^n*
                          (S*R^-3)^(n-1)*S^(n+1) )           # 25e
                , Comm( S^-2*R^4*S^2*R^3,
                        S*R^-3*S^(n-1)*R^-3*S )              # 25f
                , Comm( R^4, S^n*(S*R^-3)^(n-1) )            # 25g
                , Comm( R^4, S^-2*R^4*S^2 )                  # 25h
                , Comm( S^-2*R^4*S^2*R^-3,
                        S^n*(S*R^-3)^(n-1)*S^-2*R^4*S^2*
                          R^-3*S^n*(S*R^-3)^(n-1)         )  # 25i
                , Comm( S^-2*R^4*S^2*R^-3,
                        R^-3*S^-(n+1)*S^-2*R^4*S^2*
                          R^-3*S^(n+1)*R^3                )  # 25j
# wrong:        , Comm( S^-2*R^4*S^2*R^-3,
#                       R^-3*S^-1*R^-3*S*R^3*S^n*
#                         (S*R^-3)^(n-1)*S^-2*R^4*S^2*R^3*
#                         (S*R^-3)^(n-1)*S^n*R^-3*S^-1*
#                         R^3*S*R^3                       )  # 25k
                , Comm( S^-2*R^4*S^2*R^-3,
                        R^-3*S^-1*R^-3*S*R^3*S^n*
                          (S*R^-3)^(n-1)*S^-2*R^4*S^2*R^-3*
                          (S*R^-3)^(n-1)*S^n*R^-3*S^-1*
                          R^3*S*R^3                       )  # 25k corrected

                , R^3*(S*R^-3)^(n-1)*S^-3*R^4*S^2*R^-3*S*
                    R^3*(S*R^-3)^(n-1)*S^(n-3)*R^4*S^2*R^-3*
                    S^(n-1)*R^4*S^2*R^-3*S^(n-1)*R^3*S^-2*
                    R^-4*S^(n+3)*R^3*S^-2*R^-4*S^2           # 25l
                , (R^3*(S*R^-3)^(n-1)*S^(n-2)*R^4*S^2)^2     # 25m
                , R^12                                       # 22q
                ] ;
        # and finally the relations 25c:
        moreRels := List( [ 2 .. (n-1)/2 ],
                          i -> Comm( R^3*S^n*(S*R^-3)^(n-1),
                                     S^(-i*(n+1))*R^3*S^n*
                                     (S*R^-3)^(n-1)*S^(i*(n+1)) ) );
        fp := f / Concatenation( rels, moreRels );

        s := fp.1;  r := fp.2;
        iso := FGA_CurryAutToPQOU( r^3*s^n*(s*r^-3)^(n-1)              # 24c
                                 , s^(n+1)                             # 24a
                                 , s^n*(s*r^-3)^(n-1)                  # 24b
                                 , s^(-2*(n+1))*r^4*s^(2*(n+1))*r^-3   # 24d
                                 );

    fi;

    isoinv := GroupHomomorphismByImagesNC( fp, aut,
                                           GeneratorsOfGroup( fp ),
                                           GeneratorsOfGroup( aut ) );

    return GroupHomomorphismByFunction( aut, fp, iso,
                                        x -> x ^ isoinv );

    end );


#############################################################################
##
#F  FreeGroupEndomorphismByImages( <group>, <images> )
##
##  returns the endomorphism of <group> that maps the generators of <group>
##  to <images>.
##
InstallGlobalFunction( FreeGroupEndomorphismByImages,
    function(g,l)
    return GroupHomomorphismByImages(g,g,FreeGeneratorsOfGroup(g),l);
    end );


#############################################################################
##
#F  FreeGroupAutomorphismsGeneratorO( <group> )
#F  FreeGroupAutomorphismsGeneratorP( <group> )
#F  FreeGroupAutomorphismsGeneratorU( <group> )
#F  FreeGroupAutomorphismsGeneratorS( <group> )
#F  FreeGroupAutomorphismsGeneratorT( <group> )
#F  FreeGroupAutomorphismsGeneratorQ( <group> )
#F  FreeGroupAutomorphismsGeneratorR( <group> )
##
##  These functions return the automorphism of <group> which maps the
##  generators [<x1>, <x2>, ..., <xn>] to
##  O : [<x1>^-1 , <x2>,       ..., <xn>   ]        (n>=1)
##  P : [<x2>    , <x1>, <x3>, ..., <xn>   ]        (n>=2)
##  U : [<x1><x2>, <x2>, <x3>, ..., <xn>   ]        (n>=2)
##  S : [<x2>^-1, <x3>^-1, ..., <xn>^-1, <x1>^-1 ]  (n>=1)
##  T : [<x2>    , <x1>^-1, <x3>, ..., <xn>]        (n>=2)
##  Q : [<x2>, <x3>, ..., <xn>, <x1> ]              (n>=2)
##  R : [<x2>^-1, <x1>, <x3>, <x4>, ..., 
##       <x{n-2}>, <xn><x{n-1}>^-1, <x{n-1}>^-1]    (n>=4)
##
InstallGlobalFunction( FreeGroupAutomorphismsGeneratorO,
    function( g )
    local imgs;
    FGA_CheckRank( g, 1 );
    imgs := ShallowCopy( FreeGeneratorsOfGroup( g ) );
    imgs[1] := imgs[1]^-1;
    return FreeGroupEndomorphismByImages( g, imgs );
    end );

InstallGlobalFunction( FreeGroupAutomorphismsGeneratorP,
    function( g )
    local imgs;
    FGA_CheckRank( g, 2 );
    imgs := ShallowCopy( FreeGeneratorsOfGroup( g ) );
    imgs{[1,2]} := [ imgs[2], imgs[1] ];
    return FreeGroupEndomorphismByImages( g, imgs );
    end );

InstallGlobalFunction( FreeGroupAutomorphismsGeneratorU,
    function( g )
    local imgs;
    imgs := ShallowCopy( FreeGeneratorsOfGroup( g ) );
    FGA_CheckRank( g, 2 );
    imgs[1] := imgs[1] * imgs[2];
    return FreeGroupEndomorphismByImages( g, imgs );
    end );

InstallGlobalFunction( FreeGroupAutomorphismsGeneratorS,
    function( g )
    local imgs;
    FGA_CheckRank( g, 1 );
    imgs := FreeGeneratorsOfGroup(g){[2..Rank(g)]};
    Add( imgs, FreeGeneratorsOfGroup(g)[1] );
    return FreeGroupEndomorphismByImages( g, List(imgs, g -> g^-1) );
    end );

InstallGlobalFunction( FreeGroupAutomorphismsGeneratorT,
    function( g )
    local imgs;
    FGA_CheckRank( g, 2 );
    imgs := ShallowCopy( FreeGeneratorsOfGroup( g ) );
    imgs{[1..2]} := [ imgs[2], imgs[1]^-1 ];
    return FreeGroupEndomorphismByImages( g, imgs );
    end );

InstallGlobalFunction( FreeGroupAutomorphismsGeneratorQ,
    function( g )
    local imgs;
    FGA_CheckRank( g, 2 ); # we could allow 1
    imgs := FreeGeneratorsOfGroup(g){[2..Rank(g)]};
    Add( imgs, FreeGeneratorsOfGroup(g)[1] );
    return FreeGroupEndomorphismByImages( g, imgs );
    end );

InstallGlobalFunction( FreeGroupAutomorphismsGeneratorR,
    function( g )
    local imgs, n;
    FGA_CheckRank( g, 4 );
    n := RankOfFreeGroup( g );
    imgs := ShallowCopy( FreeGeneratorsOfGroup( g ) );
    imgs{[1,2,n-1,n]} := [ imgs[2]^-1,           imgs[1],
                           imgs[n]*imgs[n-1]^-1, imgs[n-1]^-1 ];
    return FreeGroupEndomorphismByImages( g, imgs );
    end );

#############################################################################
##
#F FGA_CheckRank( <group>, <minrank> )
##
## Checks whether <group> has rank at least <minrank>, and signals an
## error otherwise (helper function for FreeGroupAutomorphismsGenerator*)
##
InstallGlobalFunction( FGA_CheckRank,
    function( g, r )
    if RankOfFreeGroup( g ) < r then
        Error( "the rank of the group should be at least ", r );
    fi;
    return; 
    end );

#############################################################################
##
#E
```

```
from pathlib import Path
code=r'''"""Exact verification/export of Neumann's 11 Aut(F3) relators.
Input finite presentation copied from FGA AutGrp.gi; all identities checked
by reduced free words independently, before any source inference.
"""
import json
from itertools import permutations

def inv(w):return tuple(-i for i in w[::-1])
def cat(*ws):
 z=[]
 for w in ws:
  for i in w:
   if z and z[-1]==-i:z.pop()
   else:z.append(i)
 return tuple(z)
def subst(w,A):return cat(*(A[i-1] if i>0 else inv(A[-i-1]) for i in w))
def compose(A,B):return tuple(subst(w,A) for w in B)
def power(w,n):
 if n<0:w=inv(w);n=-n
 return cat(*(w for _ in range(n)))
def cm(a,b):return cat(inv(a),inv(b),a,b) # GAP commutator
I=((1,),(2,),(3,))
S,T,U=(1,),(2,),(3,)
s=lambda n:power(S,n)
t=lambda n:power(T,n)
u=lambda n:power(U,n)
rels=[power(cat(s(5),t(-1)),2),
 cat(t(-1),S,t(2),s(8),t(-1),S,t(2),s(-4)),
 power(cat(s(4),t(-1),S,t(-1)),2),t(4),
 cm(U,cat(s(2),t(-1),S,t(-1),s(2))),
 cm(U,cat(s(-2),t(-1),S,t(-1),U,s(-2),t(-1),S,t(-1))),
 cm(U,cat(s(-2),t(-1),S,T,S,t(-1),U,T,s(-1),t(-1),s(-1),T,s(2))),
 cm(U,cat(t(-1),S,t(2),U,t(-1),S,t(2))),
 cat(s(-2),t(-1),S,t(2),s(2),U,s(2),t(-1),S,t(2),s(2),U,s(-2),U,s(2),u(-1),s(-2),u(-1)),
 power(cat(s(-2),t(-1),S,T,U),2),power(cat(U,T),3)]
STU=[((-2,),(-3,),(-1,)),((2,),(-1,),(3,)),((1,2),(2,),(3,))]
# inverses for signed STU evaluation
STUi=[power((1,),1)]*3
STUi=[(( -3,),(-1,),(-2,)), ((-2,),(1,),(3,)),((1,-2),(2,),(3,))]
for a,b in zip(STU,STUi):assert compose(a,b)==compose(b,a)==I
for r in rels:
 A=I
 for x in r:A=compose((STU if x>0 else STUi)[abs(x)-1],A)
 assert A==I,(r,A)
# Same 10 Nielsen automorphisms as agent4 data, acted successively on left.
ags=[((-1,),(2,),(3,))]
ags += [tuple((i,) for i in p) for p in permutations((1,2,3)) if p!=(1,2,3)]
ags += [((2,1),(2,),(3,)),((-2,1),(2,),(3,)),((1,-2),(2,),(3,)),((1,2),(2,),(3,))]
ai={i:next(j for j,b in enumerate(ags) if compose(a,b)==compose(b,a)==I) for i,a in enumerate(ags)}
seen={I:()};front=[I]
for radius in range(1,8):
 if all(a in seen for a in STU):break
 nxt=[]
 for old in front:
  for j,g in enumerate(ags):
   A=compose(g,old)
   if A not in seen:seen[A]=seen[old]+(j,);nxt.append(A)
 front=nxt
assert all(a in seen for a in STU)
words=[seen[a] for a in STU]
expanded=[]
for r in rels:
 w=tuple(j for x in r for j in (words[x-1] if x>0 else tuple(ai[j] for j in words[-x-1][::-1])))
 A=I
 for j in w:A=compose(ags[j],A)
 assert A==I
 expanded.append(w)
if __name__=='__main__':
 print('STU Nielsen words',words)
 print('11 verified relator lengths STU',list(map(len,rels)))
 print('11 verified relator lengths Nielsen',list(map(len,expanded)))
 data={'STU_images':STU,'STU_words_in_10endos':words,'STU_relators':rels,'nielsen_relators':expanded,'convention':'0based10endoindices; append composes new automorphism on left'}
 from pathlib import Path
 Path('workspace/agent1_neumann_relators.json').write_text(json.dumps(data))
```

```
from pathlib import Path
code=r'''import json
import sympy as S
from itertools import combinations

def inv(w):return tuple(-x for x in w[::-1])
def cat(*ws):
 out=[]
 for w in ws:
  for x in w:
   if out and out[-1]==-x:out.pop()
   else:out.append(x)
 return tuple(out)
def cm(a,b):return cat(a,b,inv(a),inv(b))
def fox(word,ts):
 # Fox derivative evaluated at commutative character xi->ts[i].
 ans=[0]*len(ts);p=1
 for x in word:
  i=abs(x)-1
  if x>0:ans[i]+=p;p*=ts[i]
  else:p//=ts[i];ans[i]-=p
 return ans
X=[(1,),(2,),(3,)];gens=[];names=[]
for i in range(3):
 for j in range(3):
  if i!=j:
   A=X.copy();A[i]=cat(X[j],X[i],inv(X[j]));gens.append(A);names.append(('C',i+1,j+1))
for i in range(3):
 j,k=[a for a in range(3) if a!=i];A=X.copy();A[i]=cat(cm(X[j],X[k]),X[i]);gens.append(A);names.append(('M',i+1,j+1,k+1))
ts=[1,-1,1];mat=[]
for name,A in zip(names,gens):
 J=S.Matrix([fox(w,ts) for w in A])
 assert J*S.Matrix([0,-2,0])==S.Matrix([0,-2,0])
 assert J[0,1]==J[2,1]==0
 block=J.extract([0,2],[0,2]).T
 mat.append(block);print(name,block.tolist())
D=S.diag(-1,1);N=-S.eye(2);U=mat[6];V=mat[8]
expected=[D,S.eye(2),S.eye(2),S.eye(2),S.eye(2),N*D,U,S.eye(2),V]
assert mat==expected
assert D*U*D==U.inv() and D*V*D==V.inv()
assert U.det()==V.det()==1
# Quotient free-product representation UT4(F2).
def mul(A,B):return (A*B).applyfunc(lambda x:x%2)
def invert(A):return A.inv().applyfunc(lambda x:x%2)
I=S.eye(4);a=I.copy();a[0,1]=1;b=I.copy();b[1,2]=1;c=I.copy();c[2,3]=1
d=a;u=mul(b,a);v=mul(c,a)
assert mul(d,d)==I and mul(mul(mul(d,u),d),u)==I and mul(mul(mul(d,v),d),v)==I
images=[d,I,I,I,I,d,u,I,v]
candidate=json.load(open('workspace/agent3_cubic_candidates.json'))['candidates'][0]['word']
R=I
for x in candidate:R=mul(R,images[x-1] if x>0 else invert(images[-x-1]))
assert R!=I
print('candidate image UT4(F2)',R.tolist())
assert R==I+S.eye(4)[:,0]*S.eye(4)[3,:]
# Independently exact Johnson degree3 zero using associative derivations.
from agent1_integral_derivations import bracket
G=[]
for name in names:
 if name[0]=='C':_,i,j=name;G.append({(i-1,(j-1,i-1)):1,(i-1,(i-1,j-1)):-1})
 else:_,i,j,k=name;G.append({(i-1,(j-1,k-1)):1,(i-1,(k-1,j-1)):-1})
J1=bracket(bracket(G[8],G[0]),G[6]);J2=bracket(bracket(G[6],G[0]),G[8])
assert J1==J2
print('exact Johnson3 cancellation verified',len(J1))
'''
Path('workspace/agent1_magnus_C2_detector.py').write_text(code)
import sys;sys.path.insert(0,'workspace')
exec(compile(code,'workspace/agent1_magnus_C2_detector.py','exec'))
```

```
############################################################################
##
#O  ElementOfLpGroup( <fam>, <word> )
##
## If <fam> is the elements-family of an L-presented group and <word> is a 
## word in the free generators underlying this L-presented group, this 
## operation creates the element with the representative <word> in the 
## LpGroup with elements-family <fam>.

##

## corresponding to the generators of the L-presented group <G>.

DeclareOperation( "FreeGeneratorsOfWholeGroup", [ IsSubgroupLpGroup ] );
DeclareOperation( "FreeGroupOfWholeGroup", [ IsSubgroupLpGroup ] );

############################################################################
##
#A  FixedRelatorsOfLpGroup( <G> )
##
## returns the fixed relators of the L-presented group <G> as words
## in the free generators obtained from `FreeGeneratorsOfFpGroup'.
##
DeclareAttribute("FixedRelatorsOfLpGroup",IsLpGroup and IsGroupOfFamily);

############################################################################
##
#A  EndomorphismsOfLpGroup( <G> )
##
## returns the endomorphisms of the L-presented group <G> as group 
## homomorphisms of the underlying free group.
##
DeclareAttribute( "EndomorphismsOfLpGroup", IsLpGroup and IsGroupOfFamily );

############################################################################
##
#A  IteratedRelatorsOfLpGroup( <G> )
##
## returns the iterated relators of the L-presented group <G> as words
## in the free generators obtained from `FreeGeneratorsOfFpGroup'.
##
DeclareAttribute( "IteratedRelatorsOfLpGroup", IsLpGroup and IsGroupOfFamily );

############################################################################
##
#A  FreeGroupOfLpGroup(<G>)
##
## returns the underlying free group of the L-presented group <G>; i.e.
## the group generated by the free generators obtained from
## `FreeGeneratorsOfFpGroup'.
##
DeclareAttribute( "FreeGroupOfLpGroup", IsLpGroup and IsGroupOfFamily );

############################################################################
##
#F  LPresentedGroup ( <FreeGroup>, <rels>, <endos>, <itrels> )
##
## constructs the L-presented group on the generators of <FreeGroup> with

##
DeclareGlobalFunction( "LPresentedGroup" );

############################################################################
##
#A  UnderlyingAscendingLPresentation( <LpGroup> )
##

DeclareAttribute( "UnderlyingAscendingLPresentation", IsLpGroup );

############################################################################
##

##
## attempts to compute an underlying invariant L-presentation for the
## group <LpGroup>. In the worst case, this routine returns the underlying

## using `SetUnderlyingInvariantLPresentation'.
##
DeclareAttribute( "UnderlyingInvariantLPresentation", IsLpGroup );

############################################################################
##
#A  EmbeddingOfAscendingSubgroup ( <LpGroup> )
##
## an attribute which stores an embedding of an ascending L-presented 
## subgroup of the L-presented group <LpGroup>. This is useful for the 
## construction in FR of the Gupta-Sidki-Groups with its invariantly 
## L-presented index-3 subgroup.
##
DeclareAttribute( "EmbeddingOfAscendingSubgroup", IsLpGroup );

############################################################################
##
#O  EpimorphismFromFpGroup ( <LpGroup>, <n> )
##
## returns an epimorphism from a finitely presented group G which is obtained
## from <LpGroup> by applying word of length at most <n> in the endomorphisms 
## of the L-presented group <LpGroup>
##
DeclareOperation( "EpimorphismFromFpGroup", [ IsLpGroup, IsPosInt ] );

############################################################################
##
#O  SplitExtensionByAutomorphismsLpGroup ( <G>, <H>, <auts> )
##
## returns the split extension of <G> by <H> where the action of each 
## generator of <H> on <G> is given by the automorphisms <aut>: <H> -> <G>

##
DeclareOperation( "SplitExtensionByAutomorphismsLpGroup",

############################################################################
##
#O  AsLpGroup ( <G> )
##
## returns an L-presented group which is isomorphic to <G>

#DeclareOperation( "AsLpGroup", [ IsFreeGroup ]);
```
