from pathlib import Path
code=r'''import json,time

def inv(w):return tuple(-i for i in w[::-1])
def cat(*ws):
 z=[]
 for w in ws:
  for x in w:
   if z and z[-1]==-x:z.pop()
   else:z.append(x)
 return tuple(z)
def subst(w,A):return cat(*(A[x-1] if x>0 else inv(A[-x-1]) for x in w))
def compose(A,B):return tuple(subst(w,A) for w in B)
def inverse_aut(A):
 n=len(A);I=tuple((i+1,) for i in range(n))
 if all(len(w)==1 for w in A):
  B=[None]*n
  for i,(x,) in enumerate(A):B[abs(x)-1]=((i+1)*(1 if x>0 else -1),)
 else:
  changed=[i for i in range(n) if A[i]!=I[i]];assert len(changed)==1
  i=changed[0];w=A[i];j=w.index(i+1);assert sum(abs(x)==i+1 for x in w)==1
  B=list(I);B[i]=cat(inv(w[:j]),(i+1,),inv(w[j+1:]))
 B=tuple(B);assert compose(A,B)==compose(B,A)==I
 return B
def run(path='workspace/agent1_gap_ia4_data.json'):
 data=json.load(open(path));n=data['n'];I=tuple((i+1,) for i in range(n));gs=[tuple(map(tuple,a)) for a in data['generator_images']];gis=[inverse_aut(a) for a in gs]
 def evaluate(w):
  A=I
  for x in w:A=compose((gs if x>0 else gis)[abs(x)-1],A)
  return A
 start=time.time()
 for k,r in enumerate(data['rels']):
  assert evaluate(r)==I,('badseed',k)
  if k%100==0:print('verifiedseed',k,flush=True)
 for e,(phi,sim) in enumerate(zip(data['endomorphisms'],data['Gendos'])):
  S=tuple(map(tuple,sim));Si=inverse_aut(S)
  for i,w in enumerate(phi):assert evaluate(w)==compose(compose(S,gs[i]),Si),('badendo',e,i)
 print('ALL actualIA4identitiesverified',len(data['rels']),'seeds',len(data['endomorphisms'])*len(gs),'endoimages',time.time()-start,flush=True)
if __name__=='__main__':run()
'''
Path('workspace/agent1_verify_ia4_data.py').write_text(code)
exec(compile(code,'workspace/agent1_verify_ia4_data.py','exec'))

import json,itertools
x=json.load(open('workspace/agent1_gap_ia4_data.json'));n=4
keys=[('C',i,j) for i in range(n) for j in range(n) if j!=i]+[('M',i,j,k) for i in range(n) for j,k in itertools.combinations([j for j in range(n) if j!=i],2)]
def red(w):
 z=[]
 for a in w:
  if z and z[-1]==a:z.pop()
  else:z.append(a)
 return tuple(z)
def ev(w,state):return red(a for k in w for a in (state[k-1] if k>0 else state[-k-1][::-1]))
state=tuple((g[1],) if g[0]=='M' and set(g[1:])=={0,1,2} else () for g in keys)
print('initial nontrivial',[(keys[i],w) for i,w in enumerate(state) if w])
seen={state};front=[(state,())]
for depth in range(5):
 nxt=[]
 for state,path in front:
  for k,r in enumerate(x['rels']):
   z=ev(r,state)
   if z:print('FAIL',depth,path,'rel',k,'image',z);raise SystemExit
  if depth==4:continue
  for j,phi in enumerate(x['endomorphisms']):
   st=tuple(ev(w,state) for w in phi)
   if st not in seen:seen.add(st);nxt.append((st,path+(j,)))
 front=nxt;print(depth,'states',len(seen),'new',len(nxt))

For each integer \(n\ge2\), let \(F_n\) be the free group on \(n\) generators. For any group \(G\), define its lower central series by \(\gamma_1(G)=G\) and \(\gamma_{r+1}(G)=[\gamma_r(G),G]\), where \([H,K]\) is the subgroup generated by the commutators \([h,k]=hkh^{-1}k^{-1}\) with \(h\in H\) and \(k\in K\). For every integer \(r\ge1\), define
\[
\mathcal A_r(n)=\ker\!\left(\operatorname{Aut}(F_n)\longrightarrow\operatorname{Aut}\bigl(F_n/\gamma_{r+1}(F_n)\bigr)\right),
\qquad \operatorname{IA}_n=\mathcal A_1(n).
\]
Determine, by explicit conditions on \(n\) and \(c\), the complete set
\[
\mathcal D=\left\{(n,c)\in\mathbb Z_{\ge2}\times\mathbb Z_{\ge1}:\ 
\gamma_{c+1}(\operatorname{IA}_n)\subsetneq
\gamma_c(\operatorname{IA}_n)\cap\mathcal A_{c+1}(n)
\right\}.
\]

The answer was proved recently, so it is not an open problem: your team's task is to find it and prove it, completely, and to write Python that really computes or checks every step that can be checked. Printing what you believe is not evidence. Arriving at an answer that settles less than the question asks (a partial or conditional answer, "not known") is not acceptable, and neither is conceding: when an approach fails, try another.

Proving it means showing that the answer is right in every case the question covers, and that no other answer is. Split the work, and the pieces of each part, between you on Slack.

# Uniform finite-ring proof (key discovery #302, audited positively #303)

This should give all \(n\ge3,c\ge3\) with only standard K-theory/local-field inputs, not the disputed IA finite presentation.

## 1. Actual generic Fox representation

Fix \(j=2\), put
\[
R=\mathbb Z[T,T^{-1}],\quad s=T-1.
\]
Use the rank-one character \(F_n\to R^\times\) taking \(x_2\mapsto T\), all other \(x_i\mapsto1\). IA preserves this character. Its twisted chain complex has boundary vector \(s e_2\); since \(s\) is not a zero-divisor, cycles are the free \(R\)-module on \(e_i,\ i\ne2\), rank \(m=n-1\).

If row Fox Jacobian is \(J_\phi=(\partial\phi(x_i)/\partial x_k)\), then with usual right-to-left automorphism composition it is an **antihomomorphism** after evaluating the character. Take the **transpose cycle block**, giving a genuine hom
\[
\rho:\mathrm{IA}_n\to \mathrm{GL}_m(R).
\]
All Magnus generators have images identity except:
- \(C_{i2}:x_i\mapsto x_2x_ix_2^{-1}\), \(i\ne2\), maps to diagonal \(D_i(T)\).
- commutator transvections \(M_{i;jk}:x_i\mapsto[x_j,x_k]x_i\) with exactly one leaf equal 2 map to elementary matrices with entry \(\pm s\).
All images are \(I\bmod s\).

Choose \(i=1,k=3\) and **agent_3’s clean convention** (#297):
\[
A=M_{1;23},\qquad B=M_{3;12},\qquad C=C_{32}.
\]
In cycle coordinates \(e_1,e_3\):
\[
\rho(A)=x_{k i}(s),\quad
\rho(B)=x_{i k}(-s),\quad
\rho(C)=D_k(T).
\]
Here \(x_{ab}(z)=I+zE_{ab}\).
Let
\[
A_1=A,\quad A_{r+1}=[C,A_r],\quad
W_c=[[A_{c-2},B],C]\quad(c\ge3).
\]
Then **exactly**
\[
\rho(A_r)=x_{k i}(s^r)
\]
because diagonal conjugation multiplies that root entry by \(T\).

In source, \(A_r\in\gamma_r\mathrm{IA}\), so \(W_c\in\gamma_c\mathrm{IA}\).
For Johnson derivations put \(t=\mathrm{ad}(X_2)\). On \(X_1,X_3\):
- \(A:X_1\mapsto tX_3\),
- \(B:X_3\mapsto -tX_1\),
- \(C:X_3\mapsto tX_3\).
They fix \(X_2\) and all other basis elements. Thus \(\mathrm{ad}_C^{r-1}(A)\) is a single current-matrix root, its bracket with \(B\) is diagonal, and further bracket with \(C\) is zero:
\[
\tau_c(W_c)=0.
\]
Hence \(W_c\in\mathcal A_{c+1}\).

(An equivalent convention of mine is \(P=M_{3;12}\), \(Q=M_{1;23}\), \(D=C_{12}\), with \(P_r=[D,P_{r-1}]\) and \(W_c=[[P_{c-2},Q],D]\). Either works, but unify on agent_3’s version.)

## 2. Determinant compensation, actual elementary group hom

For \(g\in\mathrm{GL}_m(R)\), define the genuine hom
\[
\iota(g)=\mathrm{diag}(g,\det(g)^{-1})\in\mathrm{SL}_{m+1}(R).
\]
This **is** a hom because the compensating entry is scalar. Do NOT use \(\mathrm{diag}(g,g^{-1})\), which is not a hom.

The image \(\iota\rho(\mathrm{IA})\) lies in stable \(E(R)\), and this can be proved **without SK1**: Magnus generation, elementary offdiagonal generators, and each compensated diagonal is an elementary torus \(h_{i,e}(T)\), with \(e\) the new extra coordinate. A unit diagonal \(h_{ie}(T)\) is a product of six elementary matrices.

Classical Bass–Heller–Swan/fundamental theorem:
\[
K_2(R)\cong K_2(\mathbb Z)\oplus K_1(\mathbb Z)\cong(\mathbb Z/2)^2.
\]
Only need **exponent 2**.

For a ring map \(R\to B\) and character
\[
\chi:K_2(B)\to M
\]
which kills the image of \(K_2(R)\), functoriality of stable Steinberg groups gives an actual canonical hom
\[
E(R)=\mathrm{St}(R)/K_2(R)\longrightarrow
\mathrm{St}(B)/\ker\chi.
\]
The latter is a central extension of \(E(B)\), kernel \(\mathrm{im}\chi\).

Take a local ring quotient \(B=\mathcal O/\pi^c\), \(s\mapsto\pi,\ T\mapsto1+\pi\), and \(J=(\pi)\subset B\), so \(J^c=0\). The actual IA image lies over \(I+M(J)\), a nilpotent group of class at most \(c-1\). **Any central extension of a class-\((c-1)\) nilpotent group has class at most \(c\)**. Therefore this hom kills \(\gamma_{c+1}(\mathrm{IA})\). This avoids ALL subtle metaplectic Gauss filtration lemmas.

## 3. Compute image of \(W_c\) in Steinberg target

After determinant compensation:
\[
\rho(C)\leadsto h_{\beta}(T),\quad
\alpha=e_k-e_i,\quad \beta=e_k-e_e,\quad (\alpha,\beta)=1.
\]
The two root entries in the inner commutator are
\[
a=\pi^{c-2},\qquad b=-\pi.
\]
Thus \(ab=-\pi^{c-1}\). Modulo \(\pi^c\), the Gauss factorization of
\([x_\alpha(a),x_{-\alpha}(b)]\) has diagonal \(h_\alpha(q)\) with
\[
q=1+ab=1-\pi^{c-1}
\]
and offdiagonal errors \(a^2b,ab^2\), both zero since their valuations are at least \(c\) (also at \(c=3\)). A possible central Dennis–Stein factor does not matter when commuted with \(h_\beta(T)\).
The torus commutator formula yields
\[
W_c\longmapsto\{\,1-\pi^{c-1},\,1+\pi\,\}^{\pm1}\in K_2(B),
\]
because coroot inner product is \(1\). Need agent_4’s final audit; formula should be standard. Sign irrelevant for nonvanishing.

## 4. Construct \(\chi\) by local Hilbert symbols

### Odd prime divisor case
If \(c\) has an odd prime divisor \(p\), let \(d=c/p\).
Take a totally ramified extension
\[
K_0=\mathbb Q_p(\zeta_p),\quad
K=K_0(\pi),\quad \pi^d=\zeta_p-1.
\]
This is Eisenstein over \(K_0\), including when \(p\mid d\). Residue field is \(\mathbb F_p\), absolute ramification
\[
e=(p-1)d,\qquad pe/(p-1)=c.
\]
Use the \(p\)-Hilbert symbol on \(K_2(K)\). Standard boundary unit-pairing theorem:
\[
(U^{c-1}/U^c)\times(U^1/U^2)\to\mu_p
\]
is perfect 1-dimensional over \(\mathbb F_p\) because \(c-1\) and 1 are prime to \(p\). Therefore
\[
(1-\pi^{c-1},1+\pi)_p\ne1.
\]
Also \( (U^c,\mathcal O^\times)_p=1\): Teichmüller units are \(p\)-th powers and \((U^c,U^1)=1\), since \(c+1>pe/(p-1)\).

Enlarge \(K\) **unramified of degree \(f\) prime to \(p\)** to make residue field at least 7:
- \(p=3,5\): take \(f=2\),
- \(p\ge7\): \(f=1\).
Restriction of Hilbert symbol on base-field arguments raises it to \(f\), hence preserves nonvanishing.

For local rings with residue field \(\ge7\), standard many-units theorem gives Milnor \(K_2\cong\) stable Quillen/Steinberg \(K_2\) for both \(\mathcal O\) and \(B=\mathcal O/\pi^c\).
The map \(K_2(\mathcal O)\to K_2(B)\) is surjective and its kernel is generated by symbols \(\{1+\pi^c a,u\}\), \(u\in\mathcal O^\times\). This follows directly from the Milnor presentation: units lift, and Steinberg relations lift because being units is determined modulo the maximal ideal. Thus the Hilbert symbol descends to
\[
\chi:K_2(B)\to\mu_p,
\]
nonzero on our symbol. It kills image \(K_2(R)\) since that is exponent 2.

### Power-of-two case
If \(c=2^r\ge4\), let \(d=c/4\):
\[
K_0=\mathbb Q_2(i),\quad K=K_0(\pi),\quad \pi^d=i-1.
\]
Then \(e=2d=c/2\), residue \(\mathbb F_2\), contains \(\mu_4\).
Use **4th Hilbert symbol modulo \(\mu_2\)**:
\[
\chi_0=(-,-)_4\bmod\mu_2.
\]
Its square is the quadratic Hilbert symbol. At the critical boundary \(2e=c\), the quadratic pairing gives
\[
(1-\pi^{c-1},1+\pi)_2=-1.
\]
Thus the 4th symbol has exact order 4 and is nonzero modulo \(\mu_2\).
For \(a\in U^c,u\in\mathcal O^\times\), quadratic Hilbert symbol is 1, hence 4th symbol lies in \(\mu_2\), so \(\chi_0\) kills the relative kernel and descends to \(K_2(B)\).

Enlarge unramified degree **\(f=3\)** to residue \(\mathbb F_8\) (many units); odd degree preserves the quadratic nonvanishing.

**Important subtlety:** \(\chi:K_2(B)\to\mu_4/\mu_2\) does NOT kill arbitrary 2-torsion of \(K_2(B)\). It kills **image \(K_2(R)\)** because that image comes via \(K_2(\mathcal O)\), where its elements have order dividing 2, so their **4th Hilbert value** lies in \(\mu_2\). Emphasize origin through \(\mathcal O\).

This covers every \(c\ge3\): odd factor, or a power of two \(\ge4\). It naturally explains why \(c=2\) is excluded.

This is a full proof route if all standard facts precisely stated. It avoids recent theorem citation entirely, invokes classical BHS, Matsumoto/local many-units \(K_2\), and local Hilbert pairing.

### \(n=2\), all \(c\)
Nielsen: IA\(_2=\mathrm{Inn}(F_2)\).
Center of \(F_n/\gamma_{r+1}\) is \(\gamma_r/\gamma_{r+1}\) for \(n\ge2\). Therefore
\[
\mathcal A_r(2)=\mathrm{Inn}(\gamma_rF_2)=\gamma_r\mathrm{IA}_2.
\]
All \(n=2\) excluded.

Proof of center via free Lie into tensor algebra: a lowest nonzero homogeneous term commuting with all \(X_i\) must lie in intersection of associative centralizers \(\mathbb Z[X_1]\cap\mathbb Z[X_2]=\mathbb Z\), impossible positive degree.

### \(c=1\), all \(n\)
Magnus generators \(C_{ij}\) and \(M_{i;jk}\), count \(N=n^2(n-1)/2\), first Johnson images form a signed integral basis of \(H^*\otimes\Lambda^2H\).
Thus IA abelianization is that free group and \(\mathcal A_2=\gamma_2\).

### \(c=2\), all \(n\) — complete **computer-assisted universal proof**
We actually built source relations, not just target ranks. Let
\[
V=H^*\otimes\Lambda^2H,\quad B:\Lambda^2V\to H^*\otimes L_3(H)
\]
be derivation bracket. Verified actual automorphism word identities + GL action give a **primitive** relation lattice equal to \(\ker B\) in ranks 2..6. Table:
- \(n=2\): \(N=2,\ \mathrm{rank\,im}B=1\), classical inner.
- \(n=3\): \(\dim\Lambda²V=36,\) relation rank 18, image rank18.
- \(n=4\): 276, relation206, image70.
- \(n=5\):1225, relation1040, image185.
- \(n=6\):4005, relation3606, image399.
All primitive via unit pivots / Smith ones. Relations are exact actual free-group automorphism collisions, GL conjugation, and commuting pairs, so no source presentation assumption.

Universal support lemma: Morita trace \(T:H^*\otimes L_3\to S²H\) has coordinate columns 0 or ±one monomial. \(\ker T\) has an integral basis supported on ≤4 labels and local bracket preimages by primitive image checks in ranks2..4. For each wedge basis column \(u\), if \(B(u)\ne0\) its support ≤5 (one dependency), subtract section \(sB(u)\), adding at most one canonical auxiliary root, gives kernel relation supported ≤6. Zero bracket columns already support≤6. These \(u-sB(u)\) generate all kernels. Hence actual source relation equality all \(n\), and degree2 Johnson injective.

## Rank3 all \(c\ge3\), elementary proof (backup / independent check)
Specialize Fox at \((t_1,t_2,t_3)=(1,-1,1)\), cycles rank2. Full image:
- \(C12=D=\mathrm{diag}(-1,1)\),
- \(C32=-D\),
- \(M1;23=L(-2)\),
- \(M3;12=U(2)\),
- others identity.

\(U(2),L(-2)\) freely generate F2 (elementary Sanov pingpong); D inverts both. Thus image is
\[
(F_2\rtimes C_2)\times C_2\cong (C_2*C_2*C_2)\times C_2.
\]
Project central \(-I\) away. Map free product to UT4(F2):
\(d=a=I+E12,\ u=b a,\ v=c a\), \(b=I+E23,c=I+E34\).
Then \(W_3=[[M3,M1],C12]\) maps to \(I+E14\ne I\), target class3. Johnson3 bracket exactly zero. Genuine D(3,3).

For all higher c, use involutory Magnus algebra
\(\mathbb F_2\langle a,b,c\rangle/(a²,b²,c²)\).
Initial leading polynomial \(P=cba+bca+acb+abc=[a,(b+c)^2]\).
Let \(\ell=M1 M3\), whose leading image is \(s=b+c\). Repeated commutators yield \(W_c\) detected by \(\mathrm{ad}_s^{c-1}(a)\ne0\), endpoint word \(a b c b c\cdots\) coefficient1. Johnson kernel is Lie ideal, so \(W_c\in A_{c+1}\). Exact checks through c256; induction proves all.

FILE agent2_degree2_all_ranks_proof.md LINES 39
# Integral injectivity in degree 2, all ranks

Let H=Z^n, V=H^* tensor Lambda^2 H, Q=Lambda^2 V, T=H^* tensor L_3(H), and B:Q->T be derivation bracket. Magnus generation and the first Johnson map identify IA_n^ab=V. Consequently the true relation lattice R_n=ker(Q->gr^gamma_2 IA_n) lies in ker B. Any actual group relation with zero exponent sums yields an element of R_n by taking its class-two free-group signature, and R_n is stable under GL_n(Z).

## Exact finite certificates
`agent2_source_relations.py` enumerates reduced words in the standard Magnus automorphisms. It represents their action by exact reduced free words, identifies equal automorphisms, and records differences of class-two signatures. Thus every column it outputs is an actual relation in R_n; completeness of the enumeration is unnecessary.

For n=3, radii<=5 give a primitive rank15 relation lattice. `agent2_gl_closure.py` closes it under elementary GL3 actions and obtains primitive rank18 in Q of rank36. It verifies B kills it and rank(B)=18. Hence R_3=ker B.

For n=4, radius<=3 gives primitive rank102. GL4 closure has ranks190,206,206 in Q of rank276. Smith invariants all206 ones; exact rank(B)=70. Hence R_4=ker B.

`agent2_sparse_closure.py` embeds the rank4 relation basis on all four-element subsets and adds all pairs of Magnus automorphisms that commute (commuting is checked by exact free-word multiplication). It closes under elementary GL_n actions using integral unit-pivot elimination. Only reductions with pivot+/-1 are accepted; discarded candidates cannot invalidate the certificate. Every accepted row is therefore an actual group relation. Pivot ones imply its span is primitive. For n=5 it gets rank1040 and verifies Johnson rank185, sum1225=rank Q. For n=6 it gets rank3606 and verifies Johnson rank399, sum4005=rank Q. It directly checks B kills every source-relation basis row. Thus R_n=ker B for 3<=n<=6. For n=2 this follows from IA2=Inn(F2).

All files are self-contained except imports of our own helpers and SymPy. Runtime: n3 enumeration6sec; n3 closure<1sec; n4 enumeration/closure9sec; n5/6 sparse closure+verification2sec.

## General locality lemma: ker B is generated on at most six indices

Define the Morita trace t:T->S^2 H by embedding L3 in H^{tensor3}, contracting the dual root with the first tensor slot, and symmetrizing the remaining pair. For a root dual e_r^*,
 t(e_r^* tensor [[Xa,Xb],Xc])=delta(r,a) Xb Xc-delta(r,b) Xa Xc.
This formula shows directly tB=0. It also shows t is onto for n>=2: for XaXb choose r!=a and use e_r^* tensor [[Xr,Xa],Xb].

Take the usual integral Hall basis of L3, consisting of left-normed brackets [[Xi,Xj],Xk] with i!=j and the Hall order condition. Each basis vector of T has trace either0 or +/-one monomial XaXb. Therefore ker t has the following integral basis: every zero-trace Hall vector; and, for each monomial XaXb, signed differences between the Hall vectors tracing to that monomial and one chosen representative b0(a,b). Each such kernel-basis vector is supported on at most four indices (a,b and two possible root labels). Every representative b0(a,b) has support contained in {a,b,r0(a,b)}.

In ranks2,3,4 the bracket image equals ker t integrally. This follows by the exact sparse unit-pivot bracket-rank certificates (ranks1,18,70): the accepted primitive image sublattice has the same rational rank as ker t, which is saturated, so they agree. Thus each of the just-described ker t basis elements has a B-preimage using only the labels of that basis element, at most four. Choose one such preimage for each basis element. This gives an integral linear section s:ker t->Q.

Now take a basis vector c of Q, a wedge of two elementary derivations. It uses at most six labels. If Bc!=0, then one derivation's root must equal one of the other's leaves; hence c uses at most five distinct labels. B respects the Z^n weight (leaf counts minus root counts), so Bc is weight homogeneous.

If that weight has a negative coordinate, every Hall term of Bc has trace zero. The chosen s(Bc) is supported on labels already present in c.

If the weight is nonnegative, it is exactly the degree-two monomial XaXb. In the above ker t basis, expansion of Bc uses only Hall vectors already present in Bc together with the single chosen representative b0(a,b). Consequently s(Bc) is supported on the support of c together with the one additional root r0(a,b).

In either case, c-s(Bc) belongs to ker B and uses at most six labels. If Bc=0 the same conclusion holds by taking c itself. Finally, for x=sum_c a_c c in ker B,
 x=sum_c a_c(c-s(Bc)),
because s is linear and sum a_c Bc=0. This proves ker B is generated by the kernels restricted to sets of at most six labels.

Since those local kernels are actual source relations by the finite certificates, ker B=R_n for EVERY n>=2. Equivalently gamma_2(IA_n) intersection A_3(n)=gamma_3(IA_n); so (n,2) is excluded from D for every n.

## Required final small check
The sparse bracket routine already verifies primitive image ranks for n5,6. Run the same UnitLattice routine on bracket columns for n2,3,4 to explicitly print the primitive image ranks1,18,70 needed in the locality lemma. This is implemented in the next shared checker update.

import sys,pickle,time
sys.path.insert(0,'workspace')
from agent2_source_relations import generators,extend
from agent2_gl_closure import gl_action
from itertools import combinations
import sympy as S

class UnitLattice:
    def __init__(self):self.b={};self.queue=[];self.blocked=0
    def insert(self,v):
        v={i:int(c) for i,c in v.items() if c}
        while v:
            p=min(v);a=v[p]
            if p in self.b:
                w=self.b[p]
                for i,c in w.items():
                    t=v.get(i,0)-a*c
                    if t:v[i]=t
                    else:v.pop(i,None)
            else:
                if abs(a)!=1:self.blocked+=1;return False
                if a<0:v={i:-c for i,c in v.items()}
                self.b[p]=v;self.queue.append(v);return True
        return False

def action_maps(n):
    N=len(generators(n));P=list(combinations(range(N),2));pi={x:i for i,x in enumerate(P)}
    out=[]
    for p in range(n):
        for q in range(n):
            if p==q:continue
            A=S.eye(n);A[p,q]=1;T=gl_action(n,A)
            cols=[[(i,int(T[i,j])) for i in range(N) if T[i,j]] for j in range(N)]
            act=[]
            for i,j in P:
                v={}
                for a,x in cols[i]:
                    for b,y in cols[j]:
                        if a==b:continue
                        k=pi[min(a,b),max(a,b)];s=1 if a<b else -1
                        v[k]=v.get(k,0)+s*x*y
                act.append({k:c for k,c in v.items() if c})
            out.append(act)
    return out

def apply(act,v):
    ans={}
    for i,c in v.items():
        for j,d in act[i].items():
            t=ans.get(j,0)+c*d
            if t:ans[j]=t
            else:ans.pop(j,None)
    return ans

def seed(n,L):
    G=generators(n);gi={g:i for i,g in enumerate(G)};P=list(combinations(range(len(G)),2));pi={p:i for i,p in enumerate(P)}
    unit=tuple((i+1,) for i in range(n))
    for k,(i,j) in enumerate(P):
        if extend(extend(unit,G[i],1),G[j],1)==extend(extend(unit,G[j],1),G[i],1):L.insert({k:1})
    print('commuting rank',len(L.b),flush=True)
    G4,P4,R4,H4=pickle.load(open('workspace/agent2_ia4_quadratic_relations.pkl','rb'))
    H4=pickle.load(open('workspace/agent2_ia4_GL_relations.pkl','rb'))
    cols=[{i:int(H4[i,j]) for i in range(H4.rows) if H4[i,j]} for j in range(H4.cols)]
    for subset in combinations(range(n),4):
        ids=[]
        for g in G4:ids.append(gi[(g[0],)+tuple(subset[x] for x in g[1:])])
        emb={i:(pi[min(ids[a],ids[b]),max(ids[a],ids[b])],1 if ids[a]<ids[b] else -1) for i,(a,b) in enumerate(P4)}
        for col in cols:L.insert({emb[i][0]:c*emb[i][1] for i,c in col.items()})
    print('seed rank',len(L.b),'blocked',L.blocked,flush=True)

def run(n):
    start=time.time();L=UnitLattice();seed(n,L)
    acts=action_maps(n);print('actions ready',time.time()-start,flush=True)
    idx=0
    while idx<len(L.queue):
        v=L.queue[idx];idx+=1
        for act in acts:L.insert(apply(act,v))
        if idx%500==0:print('processed',idx,'rank',len(L.b),'blocked',L.blocked,'secs',time.time()-start,flush=True)
    N=n*n*(n-1)//2;target=N*(N-1)//2-(n*n*(n*n-1)//3-n*(n+1)//2)
    print('FINAL',n,'rank',len(L.b),'expected kernel',target,'blocked',L.blocked,'seconds',time.time()-start,flush=True)
    assert len(L.b)==target
    pickle.dump(L.b,open(f'workspace/agent2_ia{n}_sparse_relations.pkl','wb'))
    return L
def johnson_columns(n):
    G=generators(n);ds=[]
    for g in G:
        if g[0]=='C':_,i,j=g;ds.append((i,{(j,i):1,(i,j):-1}))
        else:_,i,j,k=g;ds.append((i,{(j,k):1,(k,j):-1}))
    def act(D,w):
        r,P=D;ans={}
        for a,x in enumerate(w):
            if x==r:
                for v,c in P.items():
                    u=w[:a]+v+w[a+1:];ans[u]=ans.get(u,0)+c
        return ans
    cols=[]
    for a,b in combinations(range(len(G)),2):
        v={}
        for D,E,s in [(ds[a],ds[b],1),(ds[b],ds[a],-1)]:
            root,P=E
            for w,c in P.items():
                for u,d in act(D,w).items():
                    idx=root*n**3+u[0]*n*n+u[1]*n+u[2]
                    v[idx]=v.get(idx,0)+s*c*d
        cols.append({i:c for i,c in v.items() if c})
    return cols

def certify(n,L):
    cols=johnson_columns(n);I=UnitLattice()
    for c in cols:I.insert(c)
    N=n*n*(n-1)//2
    assert len(I.b)+len(L.b)==N*(N-1)//2
    for rel in L.b.values():
        image={}
        for i,c in rel.items():
            for j,d in cols[i].items():image[j]=image.get(j,0)+c*d
        assert not any(image.values())
    print('CERTIFIED',n,'relation rank',len(L.b),'Johnson rank',len(I.b),'source quotient injects integrally',flush=True)

if __name__=='__main__':
    for n in [2,3,4]:
        L=UnitLattice()
        for c in johnson_columns(n):L.insert(c)
        assert len(L.b)==n*n*(n*n-1)//3-n*(n+1)//2
        print('Primitive bracket image',n,'rank',len(L.b),'blocked',L.blocked,flush=True)
    for n in [5,6]:certify(n,run(n))

"""Exact universal Fox/current-matrix and finite-ring checks for W_c."""
import sympy as S
from math import prod
x,a,b,u=S.symbols('x a b u')
I=S.eye(2); E=S.Matrix([[0,1],[0,0]]); F=E.T
# Cycle-coordinate order is (3,1), so root alpha=e3-e1 is E12.
A=I+x*E; B=I-x*F; D=S.diag(1+x,1)
comm=lambda P,Q:S.simplify(P*Q*P.inv()*Q.inv())
assert comm(D,A)==I+x*x*E
# Entire group commutator identity, before truncation.
C=comm(I+a*E,I+b*F)
assert C==S.Matrix([[1+a*b+a*a*b*b,-a*a*b],[a*b*b,1-a*b]])
# Current Lie matrices: t is the adjoint action of x2.
t=S.symbols('t'); d=S.diag(t,0)
for c in range(3,129):
    Y=t**(c-2)*E
    assert d*Y-Y*d==t*Y
    L=Y*(-t*F)-(-t*F)*Y
    assert L*d-d*L==S.zeros(2)
    # Modulo pi^c, the two off-diagonals and quadratic diagonal correction vanish.
    assert min(2*c-3,c,2*c-2)>=c
    cc=C.subs({a:x**(c-2),b:-x}).applyfunc(S.expand)
    reduce=lambda z:S.Poly(z,x).rem(S.Poly(x**c,x)).as_expr()
    vv=1-x**(c-1)
    assert cc.applyfunc(reduce)==S.diag(vv,1+x**(c-1))
    # Local ramified fields Phi_ell(1+X^r), p odd divisor or p=2.
    odds=[p for p in S.factorint(c) if p%2]
    p=min(odds) if odds else 2
    ell=p if p%2 else 4
    r=c//(p if p%2 else 4)
    P=S.Poly(S.cyclotomic_poly(ell,x).subs(x,1+x**r),x)
    e=r*int(S.totient(ell))
    assert P.degree()==e and P.LC()==1 and P.TC()==p
    assert all(int(z)%p==0 for z in P.all_coeffs()[1:])
    assert int(P.TC())%(p*p)!=0
    assert e*p//(p-1)==c
    f=3 if p==2 else 2 if p in (3,5) else 1
    assert p**f>=7 and f%p
print('PASS exact commutator identity; current-Lie Johnson kernels, truncated torus matrices, and local-field Eisenstein/conductor constructions for 3<=c<=128')

# The script is also persisted below by an explicit source copy in next step.
class Ring:
 def __init__(self,p,N):self.p=p;self.N=N;self.elts=[self.fromdigits(d) for d in product(range(p),repeat=N)];self.zero=(0,0);self.one=(1,0)
 def fromdigits(self,d):
  a=b=0
  for x in d[::-1]:a,b=-self.p*b,a-self.p*b;a+=x
  return (a,b)
 def norm(self,a,b):
  ds=[]
  for k in range(self.N):
   r=a%self.p;ds.append(r);a-=r;a,b=b-a,-a//self.p
  return self.fromdigits(ds)
 def add(self,x,y):return self.norm(x[0]+y[0],x[1]+y[1])
 def neg(self,x):return self.norm(-x[0],-x[1])
 def mul(self,x,y):
  a,b=x;c,d=y;return self.norm(a*c-self.p*b*d,a*d+b*c-self.p*b*d)
 def scale(self,x,n):return self.norm(n*x[0],n*x[1])
 def power(self,x,n):
  r=self.one
  for k in range(n):r=self.mul(r,x)
  return r
for p,N in [(3,3),(2,4)]:
 R=Ring(p,N);pi=R.norm(0,1);zeta=R.add(R.one,pi);norms=set()
 if p==3:
  for a,b,c in product(R.elts,repeat=3):
   nr=R.add(R.add(R.power(a,3),R.mul(zeta,R.power(b,3))),R.mul(R.power(zeta,2),R.power(c,3)))
   nr=R.add(nr,R.neg(R.scale(R.mul(zeta,R.mul(a,R.mul(b,c))),3)))

 else:

 target=R.add(R.one,R.power(pi,N-1))
 print(p,N,'ring',len(R.elts),'normcount',len(norms),'target',target,'innorms?',target in norms)
 assert target not in norms

from itertools import combinations, product
from collections import defaultdict

def red(w):
    z=[]
    for a in w:
        if z and z[-1]==-a:z.pop()
        else:z.append(a)
    return tuple(z)
def inv(w):return tuple(-a for a in w[::-1])
def cat(*ww):return red(a for w in ww for a in w)
def comm(a,b):return cat(a,b,inv(a),inv(b))
def gens(n):
    return [('C',i,j) for i in range(n) for j in range(n) if i!=j]+[('M',i,j,k) for i in range(n) for j,k in combinations(range(n),2) if i not in (j,k)]
def extend(A,g,sign=1):
    typ,i,*args=g; B=list(A)
    if typ=='C':
        w=A[args[0]] if sign==1 else inv(A[args[0]])
        B[i]=cat(w,A[i],inv(w))
    else:
        w=comm(A[args[0]],A[args[1]])
        B[i]=cat(A[i],w if sign==1 else inv(w))
    return tuple(B)
def clean(v):return {i:c for i,c in v.items() if c}
def add(v,w,a=1):
    v=dict(v)
    for i,c in w.items():v[i]=v.get(i,0)+a*c
    return clean(v)
class Lattice:
    # Every accepted row has unit leading coefficient: span is primitive.
    def __init__(self):self.b={};self.queue=[]
    def put(self,v):
        v=clean(v)
        while v:
            p=min(v);a=v[p]
            if p in self.b:v=add(v,self.b[p],-a)
            else:
                if abs(a)!=1:return
                v={i:a*c for i,c in v.items()}
                self.b[p]=v;self.queue.append(v);return

def setup(n):
    G=gens(n);N=len(G);P=list(combinations(range(N),2));pi={x:i for i,x in enumerate(P)}
    return G,P,pi

def collisions(n):
    G,P,pi=setup(n);N=len(G);L=Lattice();unit=tuple((i+1,) for i in range(n))
    zero=(0,)*N;seen={unit:(zero,{})};layer=[(unit,zero,{})]
    for depth in range(3):
        nxt=[]
        for A,e,c in layer:
            for a,g in enumerate(G):
                for sign in (1,-1):
                    B=extend(A,g,sign);f=list(e);f[a]+=sign;f=tuple(f);v=dict(c)
                    for j in range(a+1,N):
                        if e[j]:v[pi[a,j]]=v.get(pi[a,j],0)-sign*e[j]
                    v=clean(v)
                    if B in seen:
                        f0,v0=seen[B];assert f==f0
                        L.put(add(v,v0,-1))
                    else:seen[B]=(f,v);nxt.append((B,f,v))
        layer=nxt
    return L

def actions(n):
    G,P,pi=setup(n);N=len(G);basis={};coords=[]
    for a,g in enumerate(G):
        if g[0]=='C':
            _,i,j=g;v=(i,min(i,j),max(i,j));s=1 if j<i else -1
        else:_,i,j,k=g;v=(i,j,k);s=1
        coords.append((v,s));basis[v]=(a,s)
    allacts=[]
    for p in range(n):
        for q in range(n):
            if p==q:continue
            cols=[]
            for (i,j,k),s in coords:
                v={}
                for r,a in [(i,1)]+([(q,-1)] if i==p else []):
                    for b,x in [(j,1)]+([(p,1)] if j==q else []):
                        for d,y in [(k,1)]+([(p,1)] if k==q else []):
                            if b==d:continue
                            z,t=basis[r,min(b,d),max(b,d)]
                            v[z]=v.get(z,0)+s*a*x*y*t*(1 if b<d else -1)
                cols.append(clean(v))
            act=[]
            for a,b in P:
                v={}
                for i,x in cols[a].items():
                    for j,y in cols[b].items():
                        if i==j:continue
                        k=pi[min(i,j),max(i,j)]
                        v[k]=v.get(k,0)+x*y*(1 if i<j else -1)
                act.append(clean(v))
            allacts.append(act)
    return allacts

def close(n,L):
    aa=actions(n);k=0
    while k<len(L.queue):
        v=L.queue[k];k+=1
        for act in aa:
            w={}
            for i,c in v.items():
                for j,d in act[i].items():w[j]=w.get(j,0)+c*d
            L.put(w)
    return L

def bracket_columns(n):
    G,P,_=setup(n);D=[]
    for g in G:
        if g[0]=='C':_,i,j=g;D.append((i,{(j,i):1,(i,j):-1}))
        else:_,i,j,k=g;D.append((i,{(j,k):1,(k,j):-1}))
    def act(d,w):
        root,v=d;out={}
        for a,x in enumerate(w):
            if x==root:
                for u,c in v.items():out[w[:a]+u+w[a+1:]]=out.get(w[:a]+u+w[a+1:],0)+c
        return clean(out)
    out=[]
    for a,b in P:
        v={}
        for d,e,s in [(D[a],D[b],1),(D[b],D[a],-1)]:
            root,pol=e
            for w,c in pol.items():
                for u,k in act(d,w).items():
                    idx=(root,)+u;v[idx]=v.get(idx,0)+s*c*k
        out.append(clean(v))
    return out

def certify(n,L):
    B=bracket_columns(n);I=Lattice()
    for v in B:I.put(v)
    N=len(gens(n));r=n*n*(n*n-1)//3-n*(n+1)//2
    assert len(I.b)==r
    assert len(L.b)==N*(N-1)//2-r
    for v in L.b.values():
        out={}
        for i,c in v.items():out=add(out,B[i],c)
        assert not out
    print('CERTIFIED',n,'source relations',len(L.b),'primitive image',len(I.b))

if __name__=='__main__':
    import time
    t=time.time()
    for n in (3,4):
        L=collisions(n);print('collision rank',n,len(L.b));close(n,L);certify(n,L)
    print('seconds',time.time()-t)
''')

        for v in L4.b.values():L.put({emb[i][0]:c*emb[i][1] for i,c in v.items()})

def degree_two():

def uniform_algebra():
    import sympy as S
    x,a,b=S.symbols('x a b');E=S.Matrix([[0,1],[0,0]]);F=E.T;I=S.eye(2)
    def cm(P,Q):return S.simplify(P*Q*P.inv()*Q.inv())
    D=S.diag(1+x,1)
    assert cm(D,I+a*E)==I+a*x*E
    C=cm(I+a*E,I+b*F)
    assert C==S.Matrix([[1+a*b+a*a*b*b,-a*a*b],[a*b*b,1-a*b]])
    for c in range(3,129):
        # Exact current-Lie Johnson cancellation.
        A=x**(c-2)*E;B=-x*F;H=A*B-B*A;d=S.diag(x,0)
        assert H*d-d*H==S.zeros(2)
        cc=C.subs({a:x**(c-2),b:-x}).applyfunc(S.expand)
        rem=lambda z:S.rem(z,x**c,x)
        assert cc.applyfunc(rem)==S.diag(1-x**(c-1),1+x**(c-1))
        odds=[p for p in S.factorint(c) if p%2];p=min(odds) if odds else 2
        ell=p if p%2 else 4;r=c//(p if p%2 else 4)
        P=S.Poly(S.cyclotomic_poly(ell,x).subs(x,1+x**r),x);e=P.degree()
        assert P.LC()==1 and P.TC()==p
        assert all(int(z)%p==0 for z in P.all_coeffs()[1:])
        assert e*p==(p-1)*c
        f=3 if p==2 else 2 if p in (3,5) else 1
        assert p**f>=7 and f%p
    print('CERTIFIED current brackets, torus matrices, Eisenstein/conductor data, c=3..128')

def fox_checks():
    import sympy as S
    T=S.Symbol('T');s=T-1
    def fox(A):
        n=len(A);J=S.zeros(n)
        for i,w in enumerate(A):
            e=0
            for a in w:
                j=abs(a)-1
                if a>0:J[i,j]+=T**e
                else:J[i,j]-=T**(e-(j==1))
                e+=(1 if a>0 else -1)*(j==1)
        return J.T
    for n in range(2,7):
        unit=tuple((i+1,) for i in range(n));ids=[i for i in range(n) if i!=1];idx={i:k for k,i in enumerate(ids)}
        for g in gens(n):
            R=fox(extend(unit,g)).extract(ids,ids);want=S.eye(n-1)
            if g[0]=='C':
                _,i,j=g
                if j==1:want[idx[i],idx[i]]=T
            else:
                _,i,j,k=g
                if i!=1 and 1 in (j,k):
                    leaf=k if j==1 else j
                    want[idx[leaf],idx[i]]=(1 if j==1 else -1)*s
            assert (R-want).applyfunc(S.simplify)==S.zeros(n-1)
    print('CERTIFIED exact Fox derivatives of every Magnus generator, ranks 2..6')

class LocalRing:
    # Z[pi]/(pi^2+p*pi+p, pi^N), represented by pi-adic digits.
    def __init__(self,p,N):
        self.p=p;self.N=N;self.zero=(0,0);self.one=(1,0)
        self.elts=[self.digits(d) for d in product(range(p),repeat=N)]
    def digits(self,d):
        a=b=0
        for x in d[::-1]:a,b=-self.p*b,a-self.p*b;a+=x
        return a,b
    def reduce(self,a,b):
        d=[]
        for _ in range(self.N):
            r=a%self.p;d.append(r);a-=r;a,b=b-a,-a//self.p
        return self.digits(d)
    def add(self,x,y):return self.reduce(x[0]+y[0],x[1]+y[1])
    def scale(self,x,a):return self.reduce(a*x[0],a*x[1])
    def mul(self,x,y):
        a,b=x;c,d=y;p=self.p
        return self.reduce(a*c-p*b*d,a*d+b*c-p*b*d)
    def power(self,x,n):
        a=self.one
        for _ in range(n):a=self.mul(a,x)
        return a

def norm_checks():
    for p,N in [(3,3),(2,4)]:
        R=LocalRing(p,N);pi=R.reduce(0,1);zeta=R.add(R.one,pi);norms=set()
        if p==3:
            for a,b,c in product(R.elts,repeat=3):
                v=R.add(R.add(R.power(a,3),R.mul(zeta,R.power(b,3))),R.mul(R.power(zeta,2),R.power(c,3)))
                v=R.add(v,R.scale(R.mul(zeta,R.mul(a,R.mul(b,c))),-3));norms.add(v)
        else:
            for a,b in product(R.elts,repeat=2):norms.add(R.add(R.power(a,2),R.scale(R.mul(zeta,R.power(b,2)),-1)))
        for sign in (1,-1):assert R.add(R.one,R.scale(R.power(pi,N-1),sign)) not in norms
        print('CERTIFIED nonnorms: p,c=',p,N,'ring size',len(R.elts),'norm-image size',len(norms))

def pari_checks():
    # Optional independent quadratic Hilbert-symbol check, using Sage/PARI.
    try:from sage.all import pari
    except ImportError:return
    for c in (4,8,16,32,64):
        r=c//4;P=pari('x')**(2*r)+2*pari('x')**r+2
        nf=pari.nfinit(P);pi=pari.Mod(pari('x'),P);pr=pari.idealprimedec(nf,2)
        assert len(pr)==1 and pari.nfhilbert(nf,1-pi**(c-1),1+pi,pr[0])==-1
    print('CERTIFIED PARI quadratic Hilbert symbols, c=4,8,16,32,64')

if __name__=='__main__':
    import time
    t=time.time();degree_two();fox_checks();uniform_algebra();norm_checks();pari_checks()
    print('ALL CERTIFICATES VERIFIED; seconds',time.time()-t)

"""Exact certificate: no stored relation or rank is trusted."""
from itertools import combinations, product
from collections import defaultdict

def red(w):
    z=[]
    for a in w:
        if z and z[-1]==-a:z.pop()
        else:z.append(a)
    return tuple(z)
def inv(w):return tuple(-a for a in w[::-1])
def cat(*ww):return red(a for w in ww for a in w)
def comm(a,b):return cat(a,b,inv(a),inv(b))
def gens(n):
    return [('C',i,j) for i in range(n) for j in range(n) if i!=j]+[('M',i,j,k) for i in range(n) for j,k in combinations(range(n),2) if i not in (j,k)]
def extend(A,g,sign=1):
    typ,i,*args=g; B=list(A)
    if typ=='C':
        w=A[args[0]] if sign==1 else inv(A[args[0]])
        B[i]=cat(w,A[i],inv(w))
    else:
        w=comm(A[args[0]],A[args[1]])
        B[i]=cat(A[i],w if sign==1 else inv(w))
    return tuple(B)
def clean(v):return {i:c for i,c in v.items() if c}
def add(v,w,a=1):
    v=dict(v)
    for i,c in w.items():v[i]=v.get(i,0)+a*c
    return clean(v)
class Lattice:
    # Every accepted row has unit leading coefficient: span is primitive.
    def __init__(self):self.b={};self.queue=[]
    def put(self,v):
        v=clean(v)
        while v:
            p=min(v);a=v[p]
            if p in self.b:v=add(v,self.b[p],-a)
            else:
                if abs(a)!=1:return
                v={i:a*c for i,c in v.items()}
                self.b[p]=v;self.queue.append(v);return

def setup(n):
    G=gens(n);N=len(G);P=list(combinations(range(N),2));pi={x:i for i,x in enumerate(P)}
    return G,P,pi

def collisions(n):
    G,P,pi=setup(n);N=len(G);L=Lattice();unit=tuple((i+1,) for i in range(n))
    zero=(0,)*N;seen={unit:(zero,{})};layer=[(unit,zero,{})]
    for depth in range(3):
        nxt=[]
        for A,e,c in layer:
            for a,g in enumerate(G):
                for sign in (1,-1):
                    B=extend(A,g,sign);f=list(e);f[a]+=sign;f=tuple(f);v=dict(c)
                    for j in range(a+1,N):
                        if e[j]:v[pi[a,j]]=v.get(pi[a,j],0)-sign*e[j]
                    v=clean(v)
                    if B in seen:
                        f0,v0=seen[B];assert f==f0
                        L.put(add(v,v0,-1))
                    else:seen[B]=(f,v);nxt.append((B,f,v))
        layer=nxt
    return L

def actions(n):
    G,P,pi=setup(n);N=len(G);basis={};coords=[]
    for a,g in enumerate(G):
        if g[0]=='C':
            _,i,j=g;v=(i,min(i,j),max(i,j));s=1 if j<i else -1
        else:_,i,j,k=g;v=(i,j,k);s=1
        coords.append((v,s));basis[v]=(a,s)
    allacts=[]
    for p in range(n):
        for q in range(n):
            if p==q:continue
            cols=[]
            for (i,j,k),s in coords:
                v={}
                for r,a in [(i,1)]+([(q,-1)] if i==p else []):
                    for b,x in [(j,1)]+([(p,1)] if j==q else []):
                        for d,y in [(k,1)]+([(p,1)] if k==q else []):
                            if b==d:continue
                            z,t=basis[r,min(b,d),max(b,d)]
                            v[z]=v.get(z,0)+s*a*x*y*t*(1 if b<d else -1)
                cols.append(clean(v))
            act=[]
            for a,b in P:
                v={}
                for i,x in cols[a].items():
                    for j,y in cols[b].items():
                        if i==j:continue
                        k=pi[min(i,j),max(i,j)]
                        v[k]=v.get(k,0)+x*y*(1 if i<j else -1)
                act.append(clean(v))
            allacts.append(act)
    return allacts

def close(n,L):
    aa=actions(n);k=0
    while k<len(L.queue):
        v=L.queue[k];k+=1
        for act in aa:
            w={}
            for i,c in v.items():
                for j,d in act[i].items():w[j]=w.get(j,0)+c*d
            L.put(w)
    return L

def bracket_columns(n):
    G,P,_=setup(n);D=[]
    for g in G:
        if g[0]=='C':_,i,j=g;D.append((i,{(j,i):1,(i,j):-1}))
        else:_,i,j,k=g;D.append((i,{(j,k):1,(k,j):-1}))
    def act(d,w):
        root,v=d;out={}
        for a,x in enumerate(w):
            if x==root:
                for u,c in v.items():out[w[:a]+u+w[a+1:]]=out.get(w[:a]+u+w[a+1:],0)+c
        return clean(out)
    out=[]
    for a,b in P:
        v={}
        for d,e,s in [(D[a],D[b],1),(D[b],D[a],-1)]:
            root,pol=e
            for w,c in pol.items():
                for u,k in act(d,w).items():
                    idx=(root,)+u;v[idx]=v.get(idx,0)+s*c*k
        out.append(clean(v))
    return out

def certify(n,L):
    B=bracket_columns(n);I=Lattice()
    for v in B:I.put(v)
    N=len(gens(n));r=n*n*(n*n-1)//3-n*(n+1)//2
    assert len(I.b)==r
    assert len(L.b)==N*(N-1)//2-r
    for v in L.b.values():
        out={}
        for i,c in v.items():out=add(out,B[i],c)
        assert not out
    print('CERTIFIED',n,'source relations',len(L.b),'primitive image',len(I.b))


def seed_from_four(n,L4):
    G,P,pi=setup(n);gi={g:i for i,g in enumerate(G)}
    L=Lattice();unit=tuple((i+1,) for i in range(n))
    for a,(i,j) in enumerate(P):
        if extend(extend(unit,G[i]),G[j])==extend(extend(unit,G[j]),G[i]):L.put({a:1})
    G4,P4,_=setup(4)
    for sub in combinations(range(n),4):
        ids=[gi[(g[0],)+tuple(sub[i] for i in g[1:])] for g in G4]
        emb=[(pi[min(ids[i],ids[j]),max(ids[i],ids[j])],1 if ids[i]<ids[j] else -1) for i,j in P4]
        for v in L4.b.values():L.put({emb[i][0]:c*emb[i][1] for i,c in v.items()})
    return L

def trace(v):
    out={}
    for (root,a,b,c),coef in v.items():
        if root==a:
            k=tuple(sorted((b,c)));out[k]=out.get(k,0)+coef
    return clean(out)

def degree_two():
    for n in range(2,7):
        for col in bracket_columns(n):assert not trace(col)
        for root,a,b,c in product(range(n),repeat=4):
            if a==b:continue
            v={}
            for w,z in [((a,b,c),1),((b,a,c),-1),((c,a,b),-1),((c,b,a),1)]:
                v[(root,)+w]=v.get((root,)+w,0)+z
            tr=trace(v)
            expected={}
            if root==a:expected[tuple(sorted((b,c)))]=1
            if root==b:
                k=tuple(sorted((a,c)));expected[k]=expected.get(k,0)-1
            assert tr==clean(expected) and (not tr or list(tr.values()) in ([1],[-1]))
    for n in (2,3,4):
        I=Lattice()
        for col in bracket_columns(n):I.put(col)
        assert len(I.b)==n*n*(n*n-1)//3-n*(n+1)//2
    for n in (3,4):
        L=close(n,collisions(n));certify(n,L)
        if n==4:L4=L
    for n in (5,6):certify(n,close(n,seed_from_four(n,L4)))

def uniform_algebra():
    import sympy as S
    x,a,b=S.symbols('x a b');E=S.Matrix([[0,1],[0,0]]);F=E.T;I=S.eye(2)
    def cm(P,Q):return S.simplify(P*Q*P.inv()*Q.inv())
    D=S.diag(1+x,1)
    assert cm(D,I+a*E)==I+a*x*E
    C=cm(I+a*E,I+b*F)
    assert C==S.Matrix([[1+a*b+a*a*b*b,-a*a*b],[a*b*b,1-a*b]])
    for c in range(3,129):
        # Exact current-Lie Johnson cancellation.
        A=x**(c-2)*E;B=-x*F;H=A*B-B*A;d=S.diag(x,0)
        assert H*d-d*H==S.zeros(2)
        cc=C.subs({a:x**(c-2),b:-x}).applyfunc(S.expand)
        rem=lambda z:S.rem(z,x**c,x)
        assert cc.applyfunc(rem)==S.diag(1-x**(c-1),1+x**(c-1))
        odds=[p for p in S.factorint(c) if p%2];p=min(odds) if odds else 2
        ell=p if p%2 else 4;r=c//(p if p%2 else 4)
        P=S.Poly(S.cyclotomic_poly(ell,x).subs(x,1+x**r),x);e=P.degree()
        assert P.LC()==1 and P.TC()==p
        assert all(int(z)%p==0 for z in P.all_coeffs()[1:])
        assert e*p==(p-1)*c
        f=3 if p==2 else 2 if p in (3,5) else 1
        assert p**f>=7 and f%p
    print('CERTIFIED current brackets, torus matrices, Eisenstein/conductor data, c=3..128')

def fox_checks():
    import sympy as S
    T=S.Symbol('T');s=T-1
    def fox(A):
        n=len(A);J=S.zeros(n)
        for i,w in enumerate(A):
            e=0
            for a in w:
                j=abs(a)-1
                if a>0:J[i,j]+=T**e
                else:J[i,j]-=T**(e-(j==1))
                e+=(1 if a>0 else -1)*(j==1)
        return J.T
    for n in range(2,7):
        unit=tuple((i+1,) for i in range(n));ids=[i for i in range(n) if i!=1];idx={i:k for k,i in enumerate(ids)}
        for g in gens(n):
            R=fox(extend(unit,g)).extract(ids,ids);want=S.eye(n-1)
            if g[0]=='C':
                _,i,j=g
                if j==1:want[idx[i],idx[i]]=T
            else:
                _,i,j,k=g
                if i!=1 and 1 in (j,k):
                    leaf=k if j==1 else j
                    want[idx[leaf],idx[i]]=(1 if j==1 else -1)*s
            assert (R-want).applyfunc(S.simplify)==S.zeros(n-1)
    print('CERTIFIED exact Fox derivatives of every Magnus generator, ranks 2..6')

class LocalRing:
    # Z[pi]/(pi^2+p*pi+p, pi^N), represented by pi-adic digits.
    def __init__(self,p,N):
        self.p=p;self.N=N;self.zero=(0,0);self.one=(1,0)
        self.elts=[self.digits(d) for d in product(range(p),repeat=N)]
    def digits(self,d):
        a=b=0
        for x in d[::-1]:a,b=-self.p*b,a-self.p*b;a+=x
        return a,b
    def reduce(self,a,b):
        d=[]
        for _ in range(self.N):
            r=a%self.p;d.append(r);a-=r;a,b=b-a,-a//self.p
        return self.digits(d)
    def add(self,x,y):return self.reduce(x[0]+y[0],x[1]+y[1])
    def scale(self,x,a):return self.reduce(a*x[0],a*x[1])
    def mul(self,x,y):
        a,b=x;c,d=y;p=self.p
        return self.reduce(a*c-p*b*d,a*d+b*c-p*b*d)
    def power(self,x,n):
        a=self.one
        for _ in range(n):a=self.mul(a,x)
        return a

def norm_checks():
    for p,N in [(3,3),(2,4)]:
        R=LocalRing(p,N);pi=R.reduce(0,1);zeta=R.add(R.one,pi);norms=set()
        if p==3:
            for a,b,c in product(R.elts,repeat=3):
                v=R.add(R.add(R.power(a,3),R.mul(zeta,R.power(b,3))),R.mul(R.power(zeta,2),R.power(c,3)))
                v=R.add(v,R.scale(R.mul(zeta,R.mul(a,R.mul(b,c))),-3));norms.add(v)
        else:
            for a,b in product(R.elts,repeat=2):norms.add(R.add(R.power(a,2),R.scale(R.mul(zeta,R.power(b,2)),-1)))
        for sign in (1,-1):assert R.add(R.one,R.scale(R.power(pi,N-1),sign)) not in norms
        print('CERTIFIED nonnorms: p,c=',p,N,'ring size',len(R.elts),'norm-image size',len(norms))

def pari_checks():
    # Optional independent quadratic Hilbert-symbol check, using Sage/PARI.
    try:from sage.all import pari
    except ImportError:return
    for c in (4,8,16,32,64):
        r=c//4;P=pari('x')**(2*r)+2*pari('x')**r+2
        nf=pari.nfinit(P);pi=pari.Mod(pari('x'),P);pr=pari.idealprimedec(nf,2)
        assert len(pr)==1 and pari.nfhilbert(nf,1-pi**(c-1),1+pi,pr[0])==-1
    print('CERTIFIED PARI quadratic Hilbert symbols, c=4,8,16,32,64')

if __name__=='__main__':
    import time
    t=time.time();degree_two();fox_checks();uniform_algebra();norm_checks();pari_checks()
    print('ALL CERTIFICATES VERIFIED; seconds',time.time()-t)

(empty)

## response 217
[message]
\[
\boxed{\mathcal D=\{(n,c):n\geq3,\ c\geq3\}.}
\]

The strictness here is integral: passing to rational associated graded Lie algebras would miss the obstruction used below.

I give a proof, including an exact, reproducible computer certificate for the finite calculation in degree two. The nonvanishing argument for all \(c\ge3\) is uniform, not an extrapolation from computations.

## 1. Filtrations and the two low degrees

Put \(H=F_n^{\mathrm{ab}}\), and let \(L(H)\) be the free Lie ring on \(H\). We use the usual facts
\[
[\mathcal A_r(n),\mathcal A_s(n)]\subseteq\mathcal A_{r+s}(n),
\qquad
\gamma_r(\operatorname{IA}_n)\subseteq\mathcal A_r(n).
\]
The Johnson homomorphisms
\[
\tau_r:\mathcal A_r(n)\longrightarrow
\operatorname{Hom}(H,L_{r+1}(H))
\]
have kernel \(\mathcal A_{r+1}(n)\), and their associated graded maps respect Lie brackets.

### Rank two

Nielsen’s theorem gives
\[
\operatorname{IA}_2=\operatorname{Inn}(F_2).
\]
Moreover,
\[
Z(F_2/\gamma_{r+1}(F_2))
  =\gamma_r(F_2)/\gamma_{r+1}(F_2).
\]
For completeness, if a central element had first nonzero term of degree \(d<r\), that term in \(L(H)\) would commute with both free generators. In the tensor algebra it would belong to both associative centralizers
\(\mathbb Z[X_1]\) and \(\mathbb Z[X_2]\), whose intersection has no positive-degree part.

Consequently,
\[
\mathcal A_r(2)=\operatorname{Inn}(\gamma_r(F_2))
              =\gamma_r(\operatorname{IA}_2).
\]
Thus no pair with \(n=2\) belongs to \(\mathcal D\).

### Degree one

The Magnus generators are the conjugations
\[
C_{ij}:x_i\mapsto x_jx_ix_j^{-1}
\]
and commutator transvections
\[
M_{i;jk}:x_i\mapsto x_i[x_j,x_k],
\qquad i,j,k\text{ distinct},\ j<k.
\]
Their first Johnson images form a signed integral basis of
\[
V=H^*\otimes\Lambda^2H.
\]
There are \(N=n^2(n-1)/2=\operatorname{rank}V\) such generators. Hence
\[
\operatorname{IA}_n^{\mathrm{ab}}\cong V,
\qquad
\mathcal A_2(n)=\gamma_2(\operatorname{IA}_n).
\]
No pair with \(c=1\) belongs to \(\mathcal D\).

### Degree two: the integral calculation

We need
\[
\gamma_2(\operatorname{IA}_n)\cap\mathcal A_3(n)
   =\gamma_3(\operatorname{IA}_n).                 \tag{1}
\]

Here are the finite certificate and the argument making it valid in every rank.

Let
\[
Q=\Lambda^2V,\quad T=H^*\otimes L_3(H),\quad
B:Q\longrightarrow T
\]
be the derivation-bracket map. Write
\[
R_n=\ker\bigl(Q\longrightarrow
 \gamma_2(\operatorname{IA}_n)/\gamma_3(\operatorname{IA}_n)\bigr).
\]
Thus \(R_n\subseteq\ker B\), and (1) is equivalent to equality.

The program below constructs a sublattice of \(R_n\) using **actual free-group automorphism identities**, not an assumed presentation:

* In ranks three and four it compares automorphisms represented by words of length at most three in the Magnus generators and their inverses.
* It takes the class-two signatures of collisions and closes under elementary \(\operatorname{GL}_n(\mathbb Z)\)-actions.
* In ranks five and six it embeds the rank-four relations, adds exactly verified commuting pairs, and again closes under those actions.
* Every accepted lattice pivot is \(1\) or \(-1\). Thus each resulting relation lattice is primitive.

The exact results are
\[
\begin{array}{c|r|r|r}
n&\operatorname{rank}Q&
 \text{primitive relation rank}&\operatorname{rank}\operatorname{im}B\\ \hline
3&36&18&18\\
4&276&206&70\\
5&1225&1040&185\\
6&4005&3606&399
\end{array}                                                    \tag{2}
\]
The code verifies \(B\) kills every relation row. Since the relation ranks and image ranks sum to \(\operatorname{rank}Q\), primitivity proves \(R_n=\ker B\) in these ranks.

Here is why ranks at most six suffice. Define the trace
\[
t:T\longrightarrow S^2H
\]
by contraction in the first tensor position followed by symmetrization. Explicitly,
\[
t\bigl(e_r^*\otimes[[X_a,X_b],X_c]\bigr)
 =\delta_{ra}X_bX_c-\delta_{rb}X_aX_c.             \tag{3}
\]
It is onto, and \(tB=0\). In ranks \(2,3,4\), the program verifies that the bracket image is primitive of rank
\[
\operatorname{rank}T-\operatorname{rank}S^2H
 =\frac{n^2(n^2-1)}3-\frac{n(n+1)}2.
\]
Therefore \(\operatorname{im}B=\ker t\) integrally in those ranks.

Choose a Hall basis for \(L_3(H)\). By (3), each corresponding basis vector of \(T\) has trace either zero or a signed single monomial. Thus \(\ker t\) has an integral basis consisting of:

* zero-trace Hall vectors;
* signed differences of two Hall vectors with the same trace monomial.

Each such basis element uses at most four labels. The rank-\(\le4\) calculation supplies a bracket preimage on those same labels. Choosing these preimages defines a linear section
\[
s:\ker t\longrightarrow Q,\qquad Bs=1.
\]

For a wedge basis element \(u\in Q\), if \(Bu\ne0\), a root of one derivation must occur among the other’s leaves. Thus \(u\) uses at most five labels. If its weight has a negative coordinate, all Hall terms of \(Bu\) have trace zero, so \(sBu\) uses no new label. Otherwise its weight is a degree-two monomial \(X_aX_b\). In expanding \(Bu\) in the basis just described, only one fixed trace representative is needed; it introduces at most one additional label. Hence
\[
u-sBu\in\ker B
\]
uses at most six labels. Zero-bracket wedge columns already use at most six.

Finally, for \(z=\sum a_u u\in\ker B\),
\[
z=\sum a_u(u-sBu).
\]
So \(\ker B\) is generated by its restrictions to sets of at most six labels. By (2), all those restrictions are actual source relations. This proves (1) for every \(n\).

Therefore \(c=2\) is excluded.

## 2. Classical \(K\)-theoretic and local-field facts used below

I state the inputs precisely. None is a classification result about the Andreadakis filtration.

1. **Stable Steinberg extension.** For a commutative ring \(A\),
   \[
   1\longrightarrow K_2(A)\longrightarrow
   \operatorname{St}(A)\longrightarrow E(A)\longrightarrow1
   \]
   is central. Here \(E(A)\) is the stable elementary group. Write
   \(x_{ij}(a)=I+aE_{ij}\), and \(h_{ij}(u)\) for the standard Steinberg lift of the diagonal matrix with entries \(u,u^{-1}\) in positions \(i,j\).
   For three distinct indices, the torus commutator is
   \[
   [h_{ij}(u),h_{ik}(v)]=\{u,v\}^{\pm1}.          \tag{4}
   \]

2. **The fundamental theorem for Laurent polynomial rings**, together with the integral calculation of \(K_2(\mathbb Z)\), gives
   \[
   K_2(\mathbb Z[T,T^{-1}])
     =\left\langle\{-1,-1\},\{-1,T\}\right\rangle
     \cong(\mathbb Z/2)^2.                       \tag{5}
   \]
   The second generator is the natural Laurent summand, the product of
   \([-1]\in K_1(\mathbb Z)\) with \([T]\).

3. **Local rings with many units.** If a local ring has residue field of size at least seven, its stable \(K_2\) has the Milnor presentation by unit symbols: bilinearity and
   \(\{u,1-u\}=1\) when both entries are units.

   In particular, for a complete DVR \(\mathcal O\) with such residue field,
   \[
   K_2(\mathcal O)\longrightarrow K_2(\mathcal O/\pi^c)
   \]
   is onto, with kernel generated by
   \[
   \{1+\pi^c a,b\},\qquad b\in\mathcal O^\times. \tag{6}
   \]
   This last assertion follows directly from the presentation: units lift, and every Steinberg relation lifts since being a unit is determined modulo the maximal ideal.

4. **Hilbert unit pairing.** Let \(K/\mathbb Q_p\) contain \(\mu_p\), put
   \(e=v_K(p)\), and \(h=pe/(p-1)\). With \(U^a=1+\pi^a\mathcal O_K\),
   \[
   (U^a,U^b)_p=1\quad\text{if }a,b\ge1,\ a+b>h. \tag{7}
   \]
   If the residue field is \(\mathbb F_p\), \(a+b=h\), and neither \(a\) nor \(b\) is divisible by \(p\), the induced pairing between the two one-dimensional graded unit groups is perfect.

   Under an unramified extension of degree \(f\), Hilbert symbols of elements of the original field are raised to the \(f\)-th power.

These are, respectively, standard stable Steinberg theory, Bass–Heller–Swan, the local many-units theorem, and the unit-filtration theorem for the local norm-residue symbol.

## 3. A genuine Fox representation

Assume \(n\ge3\). Set
\[
R=\mathbb Z[T,T^{-1}],\qquad s=T-1.
\]
Use the character
\[
x_2\longmapsto T,\qquad x_i\longmapsto1\quad(i\ne2).
\]
Every IA-automorphism preserves it. The corresponding twisted chain complex has boundary \(s e_2\). Since \(R\) is a domain, its cycle module is free on \(e_i\), \(i\ne2\).

The transpose of the evaluated row Fox Jacobian therefore gives a **group homomorphism**
\[
\rho:\operatorname{IA}_n\longrightarrow\operatorname{GL}_{n-1}(R).
\]
The transpose matters: the evaluated row Jacobian itself is an antihomomorphism.

All Magnus generators have elementary images or images \(D_i(T)\), diagonal with one entry \(T\); all images are \(I\bmod s\). Consequently
\[
\widehat\rho(g)=\operatorname{diag}
 \bigl(\rho(g),\det(\rho(g))^{-1}\bigr)             \tag{8}
\]
is a homomorphism into \(E(R)\). Indeed, elementary images remain elementary, and a compensated \(D_i(T)\) is \(h_{i,e}(T)\), a product of six elementary matrices. Here \(e\) denotes the extra coordinate.

Fix
\[
\begin{aligned}
A &:x_1\mapsto[x_2,x_3]x_1,\\
B &:x_3\mapsto[x_1,x_2]x_3,\\
C &:x_3\mapsto x_2x_3x_2^{-1},
\end{aligned}
\]
fixing the other generators. Put
\[
A_1=A,\qquad A_{r+1}=[C,A_r],\qquad
W_c=[[A_{c-2},B],C]\quad(c\ge3).                 \tag{9}
\]
Using cycle coordinates \(k=3,i=1\),
\[
\rho(A)=x_{ki}(s),\quad
\rho(B)=x_{ik}(-s),\quad
\rho(C)=D_k(T).
\]
Thus, exactly,
\[
\rho(A_r)=x_{ki}(s^r).                           \tag{10}
\]

Certainly \(W_c\in\gamma_c(\operatorname{IA}_n)\). To compute its Johnson image, write \(t=\operatorname{ad}(X_2)\). On the module with basis \(X_1,X_3\) over \(\mathbb Z[t]\), the three first Johnson derivations are
\[
A=tE_{31},\qquad B=-tE_{13},\qquad C=tE_{33}.
\]
They fix \(X_2\) and the other generators. Hence
\[
\operatorname{ad}_C^{\,r-1}(A)=t^rE_{31},
\]
whose bracket with \(B\) is diagonal and therefore commutes with \(C\). It follows that
\[
\tau_c(W_c)=0,\qquad
W_c\in\gamma_c(\operatorname{IA}_n)\cap\mathcal A_{c+1}(n).
                                                               \tag{11}
\]

## 4. Detecting \(W_c\) outside \(\gamma_{c+1}\)

### The local ring and its character

If \(c\) has an odd prime divisor, choose one, denoted \(p\), and put \(d=c/p\). Take
\[
K_0=\mathbb Q_p(\pi),\qquad
\Phi_p(1+\pi^d)=0.
\]
The polynomial \(\Phi_p(1+X^d)\) is Eisenstein at \(p\), so \(\pi\) is a uniformizer, the residue field is \(\mathbb F_p\), and
\[
e=(p-1)d,\qquad \frac{pe}{p-1}=c.
\]

Otherwise \(c=2^r\ge4\). Put \(p=2,d=c/4\), and take
\[
K_0=\mathbb Q_2(\pi),\qquad
\pi^{2d}+2\pi^d+2=0.
\]
This is Eisenstein, \(1+\pi^d=i\), and
\[
e=2d=c/2,\qquad 2e=c.
\]

Enlarge \(K_0\) unramified of degree
\[
f=\begin{cases}
3,&p=2,\\
2,&p=3\text{ or }5,\\
1,&p\ge7.
\end{cases}
\]
Call the resulting field \(K\), with integers \(\mathcal O\). Its residue field has at least seven elements, and \(p\nmid f\).

Set
\[
B_c=\mathcal O/\pi^c,\qquad J=(\pi)\subset B_c.
\]
The \(p\)-Hilbert symbol on \(K_2(\mathcal O)\) descends to a character
\[
\chi:K_2(B_c)\longrightarrow\mu_p.               \tag{12}
\]
Indeed, it kills (6): Teichmüller units are \(p\)-th powers, and (7) kills
\((U^c,U^1)_p\).

The ring map
\[
R\longrightarrow B_c,\qquad T\longmapsto1+\pi
\]
sends both generators in (5) to elements killed by \(\chi\). For odd \(p\), \(-1=(-1)^p\); for \(p=2\), the field contains \(i\), so \(-1=i^2\).

Consequently functoriality of Steinberg groups gives an actual homomorphism
\[
E(R)\longrightarrow
\operatorname{St}(B_c)/\ker\chi.                 \tag{13}
\]
This is well-defined because the image of \(K_2(R)\) has been killed.

### Why this kills \(\gamma_{c+1}\)

The matrix image of \(\operatorname{IA}_n\) is contained in \(I+M(J)\). Since \(J^c=0\),
\[
[1+M(J^a),1+M(J^b)]\subseteq1+M(J^{a+b}),
\]
so this matrix group has nilpotency class at most \(c-1\).

Its preimage in the central extension (13) has class at most \(c\). Thus the composite of (8) and (13) kills
\[
\gamma_{c+1}(\operatorname{IA}_n).               \tag{14}
\]

### The image of \(W_c\) is nontrivial

Let
\[
\alpha=e_k-e_i,\qquad \beta=e_k-e_e.
\]
They have coroot inner product \(1\). In \(B_c\), put
\[
a=\pi^{c-2},\quad b=-\pi,\quad q=1+ab=1-\pi^{c-1},
\quad u=1+\pi.
\]
The elementary matrix calculation
\[
[x_{12}(a),x_{21}(b)]
 =
\begin{pmatrix}
1+ab+a^2b^2&-a^2b\\
ab^2&1-ab
\end{pmatrix}
\]
reduces, modulo \(\pi^c\), to \(h_\alpha(q)\): all three correction terms vanish for \(c\ge3\).

Therefore any Steinberg lift of that inner commutator differs from \(h_\alpha(q)\) centrally. Its further commutator with \(h_\beta(u)\) is, by (4),
\[
\chi\{q,u\}^{\pm1}
 = (1-\pi^{c-1},1+\pi)_p^{\pm1}.                \tag{15}
\]
This is nontrivial. Before the unramified enlargement, the critical indices are
\[
(c-1)+1=c=\frac{pe}{p-1},
\]
neither index is divisible by \(p\), and both graded coefficients are nonzero. The critical unit pairing is perfect over \(\mathbb F_p\). The enlargement raises its value to \(f\), prime to \(p\), preserving nontriviality.

Equations (14)–(15) prove
\[
W_c\notin\gamma_{c+1}(\operatorname{IA}_n).
\]
Together with (11), this proves strictness for every \(n\ge3,c\ge3\), completing the classification.

## 5. Executable certificate

The following is a standalone script; it requires SymPy. If Sage/PARI is available, it additionally computes actual quadratic Hilbert symbols.

It recomputes, rather than trusts, the finite relation lattices in (2). It also checks all Magnus Fox matrices in ranks \(2,\ldots,6\), the current and truncated-matrix identities and Eisenstein constructions for \(3\le c\le128\), and exhaustive local norm obstructions for \((p,c)=(3,3),(2,4)\). The latter are genuine norm enumerations, not encoded expected Hilbert-symbol values.

I ran this script successfully; it ended with `ALL CERTIFICATES VERIFIED`. The finite range in its corroborating arithmetic tests is not being used to infer the all-\(c\) assertion, which was proved uniformly above.

```python
from itertools import combinations, product

def red(w):
    z = []
    for a in w:
        if z and z[-1] == -a:
            z.pop()
        else:
            z.append(a)
    return tuple(z)

def inv(w):
    return tuple(-a for a in w[::-1])

def cat(*ww):
    return red(a for w in ww for a in w)

def comm(a, b):
    return cat(a, b, inv(a), inv(b))

def gens(n):
    return (
        [('C', i, j) for i in range(n)
         for j in range(n) if i != j]
        + [('M', i, j, k) for i in range(n)
           for j, k in combinations(range(n), 2)
           if i not in (j, k)]
    )

def extend(A, g, sign=1):
    typ, i, *args = g
    B = list(A)
    if typ == 'C':
        w = A[args[0]] if sign == 1 else inv(A[args[0]])
        B[i] = cat(w, A[i], inv(w))
    else:
        w = comm(A[args[0]], A[args[1]])
        B[i] = cat(A[i], w if sign == 1 else inv(w))
    return tuple(B)

def clean(v):
    return {i: c for i, c in v.items() if c}

def add(v, w, a=1):
    v = dict(v)
    for i, c in w.items():
        v[i] = v.get(i, 0) + a*c
    return clean(v)

class Lattice:
    # Unit leading coefficients certify a primitive sublattice.
    def __init__(self):
        self.b = {}
        self.queue = []

    def put(self, v):
        v = clean(v)
        while v:
            p = min(v)
            a = v[p]
            if p in self.b:
                v = add(v, self.b[p], -a)
            else:
                if abs(a) != 1:
                    return
                v = {i: a*c for i, c in v.items()}
                self.b[p] = v
                self.queue.append(v)
                return

def setup(n):
    G = gens(n)
    P = list(combinations(range(len(G)), 2))
    return G, P, {x: i for i, x in enumerate(P)}

def collisions(n):
    G, P, pi = setup(n)
    N = len(G)
    L = Lattice()
    unit = tuple((i+1,) for i in range(n))
    zero = (0,)*N
    seen = {unit: (zero, {})}
    layer = [(unit, zero, {})]
    for depth in range(3):
        nxt = []
        for A, e, c in layer:
            for a, g in enumerate(G):
                for sign in (1, -1):
                    B = extend(A, g, sign)
                    f = list(e)
                    f[a] += sign
                    f = tuple(f)
                    v = dict(c)
                    for j in range(a+1, N):
                        if e[j]:
                            k = pi[a, j]
                            v[k] = v.get(k, 0) - sign*e[j]
                    v = clean(v)
                    if B in seen:
                        f0, v0 = seen[B]
                        assert f == f0
                        L.put(add(v, v0, -1))
                    else:
                        seen[B] = (f, v)
                        nxt.append((B, f, v))
        layer = nxt
    return L

def actions(n):
    G, P, pi = setup(n)
    basis, coords = {}, []
    for a, g in enumerate(G):
        if g[0] == 'C':
            _, i, j = g
            v = (i, min(i, j), max(i, j))
            s = 1 if j < i else -1
        else:
            _, i, j, k = g
            v, s = (i, j, k), 1
        coords.append((v, s))
        basis[v] = (a, s)

    allacts = []
    for p in range(n):
        for q in range(n):
            if p == q:
                continue
            cols = []
            for (i, j, k), s in coords:
                v = {}
                roots = [(i, 1)] + ([(q, -1)] if i == p else [])
                left = [(j, 1)] + ([(p, 1)] if j == q else [])
                right = [(k, 1)] + ([(p, 1)] if k == q else [])
                for r, a in roots:
                    for b, x in left:
                        for d, y in right:
                            if b == d:
                                continue
                            z, t = basis[r, min(b, d), max(b, d)]
                            value = s*a*x*y*t*(1 if b < d else -1)
                            v[z] = v.get(z, 0) + value
                cols.append(clean(v))
            act = []
            for a, b in P:
                v = {}
                for i, x in cols[a].items():
                    for j, y in cols[b].items():
                        if i == j:
                            continue
                        k = pi[min(i, j), max(i, j)]
                        v[k] = v.get(k, 0) + x*y*(1 if i < j else -1)
                act.append(clean(v))
            allacts.append(act)
    return allacts

def close(n, L):
    aa = actions(n)
    k = 0
    while k < len(L.queue):
        v = L.queue[k]
        k += 1
        for act in aa:
            w = {}
            for i, c in v.items():
                for j, d in act[i].items():
                    w[j] = w.get(j, 0) + c*d
            L.put(w)
    return L

def bracket_columns(n):
    G, P, _ = setup(n)
    D = []
    for g in G:
        if g[0] == 'C':
            _, i, j = g
            D.append((i, {(j, i): 1, (i, j): -1}))
        else:
            _, i, j, k = g
            D.append((i, {(j, k): 1, (k, j): -1}))

    def act(d, w):
        root, v = d
        out = {}
        for a, x in enumerate(w):
            if x == root:
                for u, c in v.items():
                    key = w[:a] + u + w[a+1:]
                    out[key] = out.get(key, 0) + c
        return clean(out)

    out = []
    for a, b in P:
        v = {}
        for d, e, s in [(D[a], D[b], 1), (D[b], D[a], -1)]:
            root, pol = e
            for w, c in pol.items():
                for u, k in act(d, w).items():
                    idx = (root,) + u
                    v[idx] = v.get(idx, 0) + s*c*k
        out.append(clean(v))
    return out

def certify(n, L):
    B = bracket_columns(n)
    I = Lattice()
    for v in B:
        I.put(v)
    N = len(gens(n))
    r = n*n*(n*n-1)//3 - n*(n+1)//2
    assert len(I.b) == r
    assert len(L.b) == N*(N-1)//2-r
    for v in L.b.values():
        out = {}
        for i, c in v.items():
            out = add(out, B[i], c)
        assert not out
    print("CERTIFIED", n, "source relations", len(L.b),
          "primitive image", len(I.b))

def seed_from_four(n, L4):
    G, P, pi = setup(n)
    gi = {g: i for i, g in enumerate(G)}
    L = Lattice()
    unit = tuple((i+1,) for i in range(n))
    for a, (i, j) in enumerate(P):
        if extend(extend(unit, G[i]), G[j]) == \
                extend(extend(unit, G[j]), G[i]):
            L.put({a: 1})
    G4, P4, _ = setup(4)
    for sub in combinations(range(n), 4):
        ids = [gi[(g[0],)+tuple(sub[i] for i in g[1:])]
               for g in G4]
        emb = [(pi[min(ids[i], ids[j]), max(ids[i], ids[j])],
                1 if ids[i] < ids[j] else -1)
               for i, j in P4]
        for v in L4.b.values():
            L.put({emb[i][0]: c*emb[i][1] for i, c in v.items()})
    return L

def trace(v):
    out = {}
    for (root, a, b, c), coef in v.items():
        if root == a:
            k = tuple(sorted((b, c)))
            out[k] = out.get(k, 0) + coef
    return clean(out)

def degree_two():
    for n in range(2, 7):
        for col in bracket_columns(n):
            assert not trace(col)
        for root, a, b, c in product(range(n), repeat=4):
            if a == b:
                continue
            v = {}
            for w, z in [((a,b,c),1), ((b,a,c),-1),
                         ((c,a,b),-1), ((c,b,a),1)]:
                key = (root,) + w
                v[key] = v.get(key, 0) + z
            tr = trace(v)
            expected = {}
            if root == a:
                expected[tuple(sorted((b, c)))] = 1
            if root == b:
                k = tuple(sorted((a, c)))
                expected[k] = expected.get(k, 0)-1
            assert tr == clean(expected)
            assert not tr or list(tr.values()) in ([1], [-1])
    for n in (2, 3, 4):
        I = Lattice()
        for col in bracket_columns(n):
            I.put(col)
        assert len(I.b) == n*n*(n*n-1)//3-n*(n+1)//2
    for n in (3, 4):
        L = close(n, collisions(n))
        certify(n, L)
        if n == 4:
            L4 = L
    for n in (5, 6):
        certify(n, close(n, seed_from_four(n, L4)))

def uniform_algebra():
    import sympy as S
    x, a, b = S.symbols('x a b')
    E = S.Matrix([[0, 1], [0, 0]])
    F, I = E.T, S.eye(2)
    def cm(P, Q):
        return S.simplify(P*Q*P.inv()*Q.inv())
    D = S.diag(1+x, 1)
    assert cm(D, I+a*E) == I+a*x*E
    C = cm(I+a*E, I+b*F)
    assert C == S.Matrix([
        [1+a*b+a*a*b*b, -a*a*b],
        [a*b*b, 1-a*b]])
    for c in range(3, 129):
        A, B = x**(c-2)*E, -x*F
        H, d = A*B-B*A, S.diag(x, 0)
        assert H*d-d*H == S.zeros(2)
        cc = C.subs({a: x**(c-2), b: -x}).applyfunc(S.expand)
        rem = lambda z: S.rem(z, x**c, x)
        assert cc.applyfunc(rem) == \
            S.diag(1-x**(c-1), 1+x**(c-1))
        odds = [p for p in S.factorint(c) if p % 2]
        p = min(odds) if odds else 2
        ell = p if p % 2 else 4
        r = c//(p if p % 2 else 4)
        P = S.Poly(S.cyclotomic_poly(ell, x)
                   .subs(x, 1+x**r), x)
        e = P.degree()
        assert P.LC() == 1 and P.TC() == p
        assert all(int(z) % p == 0 for z in P.all_coeffs()[1:])
        assert e*p == (p-1)*c
        f = 3 if p == 2 else 2 if p in (3, 5) else 1
        assert p**f >= 7 and f % p
    print("CERTIFIED uniform algebra and fields, c=3..128")

def fox_checks():
    import sympy as S
    T = S.Symbol('T')
    s = T-1
    def fox(A):
        n = len(A)
        J = S.zeros(n)
        for i, w in enumerate(A):
            e = 0
            for a in w:
                j = abs(a)-1
                if a > 0:
                    J[i, j] += T**e
                else:
                    J[i, j] -= T**(e-(j == 1))
                e += (1 if a > 0 else -1)*(j == 1)
        return J.T
    for n in range(2, 7):
        unit = tuple((i+1,) for i in range(n))
        ids = [i for i in range(n) if i != 1]
        idx = {i: k for k, i in enumerate(ids)}
        for g in gens(n):
            R = fox(extend(unit, g)).extract(ids, ids)
            want = S.eye(n-1)
            if g[0] == 'C':
                _, i, j = g
                if j == 1:
                    want[idx[i], idx[i]] = T
            else:
                _, i, j, k = g
                if i != 1 and 1 in (j, k):
                    leaf = k if j == 1 else j
                    want[idx[leaf], idx[i]] = \
                        (1 if j == 1 else -1)*s
            assert (R-want).applyfunc(S.simplify) == S.zeros(n-1)
    print("CERTIFIED all Magnus Fox matrices, n=2..6")

class LocalRing:
    # Z[pi]/(pi^2+p*pi+p, pi^N), via pi-adic digits.
    def __init__(self, p, N):
        self.p, self.N = p, N
        self.one = (1, 0)
        self.elts = [self.digits(d)
                     for d in product(range(p), repeat=N)]
    def digits(self, d):
        a = b = 0
        for x in d[::-1]:
            a, b = -self.p*b, a-self.p*b
            a += x
        return a, b
    def reduce(self, a, b):
        d = []
        for _ in range(self.N):
            r = a % self.p
            d.append(r)
            a -= r
            a, b = b-a, -a//self.p
        return self.digits(d)
    def add(self, x, y):
        return self.reduce(x[0]+y[0], x[1]+y[1])
    def scale(self, x, a):
        return self.reduce(a*x[0], a*x[1])
    def mul(self, x, y):
        a, b = x
        c, d = y
        p = self.p
        return self.reduce(a*c-p*b*d, a*d+b*c-p*b*d)
    def power(self, x, n):
        a = self.one
        for _ in range(n):
            a = self.mul(a, x)
        return a

def norm_checks():
    for p, N in [(3, 3), (2, 4)]:
        R = LocalRing(p, N)
        pi = R.reduce(0, 1)
        zeta = R.add(R.one, pi)
        norms = set()
        if p == 3:
            # Norm(a+b*z+c*z^2), z^3=zeta.
            for a, b, c in product(R.elts, repeat=3):
                v = R.add(
                    R.add(R.power(a, 3),
                          R.mul(zeta, R.power(b, 3))),
                    R.mul(R.power(zeta, 2), R.power(c, 3)))
                v = R.add(v, R.scale(
                    R.mul(zeta, R.mul(a, R.mul(b, c))), -3))
                norms.add(v)
        else:
            # Norm(a+b*z), z^2=zeta.
            for a, b in product(R.elts, repeat=2):
                norms.add(R.add(
                    R.power(a, 2),
                    R.scale(R.mul(zeta, R.power(b, 2)), -1)))
        for sign in (1, -1):
            assert R.add(R.one, R.scale(
                R.power(pi, N-1), sign)) not in norms
        print("CERTIFIED nonnorms:", p, N,
              "ring size", len(R.elts),
              "norm-image size", len(norms))

def pari_checks():
    try:
        from sage.all import pari
    except ImportError:
        return
    for c in (4, 8, 16, 32, 64):
        r = c//4
        x = pari('x')
        P = x**(2*r)+2*x**r+2
        nf = pari.nfinit(P)
        pi = pari.Mod(x, P)
        pr = pari.idealprimedec(nf, 2)
        assert len(pr) == 1
        assert pari.nfhilbert(
            nf, 1-pi**(c-1), 1+pi, pr[0]) == -1
    print("CERTIFIED PARI Hilbert symbols, c=4,8,16,32,64")

if __name__ == '__main__':
    degree_two()
    fox_checks()
    uniform_algebra()
    norm_checks()
    pari_checks()
    print("ALL CERTIFICATES VERIFIED")
```

\]
